## 2026-10-23 — #2498 W4-05: the per-file coverage redundancy matrix and the source-reader triage

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2498-test-pyramid -->

#2498 W4-05. v5.35.0 Wave 4 chunk, plan r2 (W4-01 to W4-04 ticked at dispatch); Refs #2498 and #2371, both open, #2362 untouched. Built on `feat/2498-w4-05-redundancy-audits` from `origin/main` `f0882cae` under the Integrator's W4-05 release (queue rev 228, readback token `W4-05-READBACK-20261023T031435Z-F0882CAE`), on Opus 5.5 after the Operator's model switch. The two audits #2498 Phase 2 asks for, as reports (D4: no test deleted or changed).

**The redundancy matrix.** `scripts/audit-test-redundancy.js` runs each file of a tier (`--tier`) or a named list alone, under its tier's guard and timeout from `scripts/test-stage.js`, with `--experimental-test-coverage --test-reporter=lcov` (zero dependencies, so no `c8`), and keeps each file's product lines: `server.js`, `lib/`, `public/`, `bin/`, `scripts/` lines with a hit count above zero, never the test's own or its helpers'. It reports what each file adds, the exact-subset candidates (add nothing), one greedy set of them retirable together without losing a line (twins are each a candidate, only one goes), and the files that execute no product line. A failed, silent or unparseable run is named, left out (which can only make others look less redundant) and exits 1; runs that all succeed with no product line among them are refused by name (Critic R-2: lcov paths in a form the filter does not match would otherwise read as a tier proving nothing, exit 0). The lcov parse is pinned on a report shaped as node 22 writes it, with every malformed or misplaced record refused by line. Measured on the Small tier at `f0882cae`: 249 files in 14.1 s, 5 at a time; 28 candidates, 27 removable together, 90 executing no product line, 107,170 product lines in all. Many candidates execute tens of thousands of lines only by loading a large module, which is the stated limit of line coverage and why the list is candidates, not deletions.

**The source-reader triage.** `scripts/audit-source-readers.js` reuses the W4-04 walker's masking and path resolution to find every `readFileSync`/`readFile` of product source on the file system: bare (destructured), on any name the test binds to `fs`/`fs/promises` (`.promises` included), or on an inline `require('node:fs')`, with the path a literal, a root-constant join (an inline `require('node:path').join` too), a `require.resolve('./…')` or a constant; a helper reading a directory by a literal file name reads that file, a sweep when it names none; a read on any other receiver, or of a path it cannot resolve, is opaque (counted per file, never classified). Classes: guard (non-code files or a sweep; kept), lift (`test/helpers/lift.js` users; kept), regex-of-behaviour (a named `.js` file or `bin/` command), split by runtime proof from the require graph plus spawned entry points: self, other, none; `public/` never counts as loaded. Measured on this chunk's tree in 1.3 s: 233 readers, 12 guard, 81 lift, 140 regex-of-behaviour (47 self, 22 other, 71 none, nearly all `public/`), 827 opaque reads in 168 files. Three rule corrections came from reading the real tree before the tests were written: a helper like `pub('app.js')` had read as a sweep (filed as a kept guard), a root read naming only a document had read as a sweep, and `bin/` commands have no `.js` extension and had read as non-code. The Critic found a fourth (R-1 below): reads on a renamed `fs` binding (`fsMod`, `fsNode`, `fsx`) and on an inline `require('node:fs')` were dropped, neither read nor opaque, and the count was 224 before the fix.

**Not the plan's 37.** The plan's W4-05 line names "the 37 measured readers"; neither the plan nor #2498 (207 on its 2026-10-18 snapshot) recorded the rule behind its number. The triage states its rule in the script and on `docs/test-pyramid.md`, and its 233 is by that rule. No requirement moves: the deliverable is the classification, and every reader is classified.

**Follow-ups and records.** Filed after searching open issues: #2548 (the matrix's first consolidation batch) and #2549 (the 69 `self` and `other` readers of `lib/`, `server.js`, `bin/`); the `none` group is `public/` and #1037's territory, cited rather than duplicated. `test/tiers.json` gains the two new test files under `small` (PM ruling via INT: the sole file-list delta, also named in the plan header's standing list). Fragment `changelog.d/2498.w4-05-redundancy-audits.internal.md`; CHANGELOG and FEATURES entries in the PR body for the records PR. Not touched: `.github/workflows/test.yml`, `.prawduct/project-state.yaml`, every gate and rule, every existing test; `PRODUCT_DIRS` in `scripts/_require-graph.js` stays for W4-06.

## 2026-10-22 — #2498 W4-04: Stage 2, the touched-graph selector that fails closed, and the dead-export audit with its ratchet

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2498-test-pyramid -->

#2498 W4-04. v5.35.0 Wave 4 chunk, plan r2 (W4-01 to W4-03 ticked at dispatch); Refs #2498 and #2371, both open, #2362 untouched. Built on `feat/2498-w4-04-touched-graph` from `origin/main` `68c4ff0e` under the Integrator's W4-04 dispatch (queue rev 214, restart token `W4-04-20261022T2105Z-68C4FF0E-D22B5E`), the fourth chunk of the five-stage test pyramid: the two require-graph tools on one walker, which tests to run for a diff, and which `lib/` exports nothing reads.

**The walker.** `scripts/_require-graph.js` (a tooling script under `scripts/**`, A416 R1) reads a file's code with strings, comments and regex bodies masked first, resolves `require('./x')` as node does and `require(path.join(ROOT, 'lib', 'x'))` through the file's own root constant (every script here loads `lib/` that way; 55 such requires on `main` resolve as literals), reports a dynamic require with the directory its literal prefix names or with no bound, takes a product path the text names (a source read, a spawned script, a bare literal on disk) as a dependency and walks a named `.js` in turn (a spawned script's requires are the test's), a run-time last segment as a dependency on the directory, and counts a read with nothing literal as opaque. Exports are read from `module.exports = {…}`, `module.exports = value` and `exports.x =`; a reader's use of a module from the shape of its require. Every function takes an io seam, and `test/require-graph.test.js` proves it on an in-memory tree.

**Stage 2.** `scripts/test-touched.js` selects the tests whose graph or named paths reach a changed file (diff against the merge-base with `origin/main` or `--base`, plus the working tree), adds changed tests and the static ring, and with `--run`, or as `node scripts/test-stage.js 2`, runs each tier under its guard with one junit per tier. It fails closed (A416 R4): `dynamic-require`, `not-javascript`, `shared-helper` and `unreached` each exit 1 with the reason named and `ESCALATE: run stage 3`, and the runner then runs Stage 3 whole and says so; no flag accepts a partial selection. `test/test-touched.test.js` pins each reason, the one-file selection, the parser; `test/test-stage.test.js` pins the wiring on a fixture repository (one tier run under its guard, the escalation into Stage 3, a red selection a red stage). Measured here: graph 1,086 files in about 1.1 s; a one-file `lib/test-results.js` change selects 18 tests plus the ring and runs in 41.4 s (Small 0.3 s, Medium 10.5 s, Large 29.5 s); this chunk's own diff escalates on its non-JavaScript files and Stage 3 runs whole in 4.9 s. The Critic's one observation on the first commit (a script a test spawns by path but never requires was not walked, so its `lib/` requires were invisible to the selection) is fixed in the second, which also caught a latent walk bug the new case exposed (a file reached through a named path was marked seen before it was popped, so its own requires were never followed), and narrows the walker's and the audit's file-system catches to the absence codes they mean.

**The audit and the ratchet.** `scripts/audit-dead-exports.js` walks from `server.js`, `bin/tc`, `bin/tc-bridge-helper`, `bin/tc-bridge-receive`, `scripts/*.js`, `test/*.test.js` and every `lib/` module, and reports dead exports, test-only exports with their readers, orphan candidates, and what it cannot audit (opaque modules, modules shadowed by a bounded dynamic require, unbounded dynamic requires). On a clean `git archive` of `68c4ff0e`: 294 modules, 3,251 names, 234 dead, 844 test-only, 163 orphan candidates, 30 `lib/wrap-steps/` modules shadowed by `test/wrap-pipeline.test.js:923`, 0 opaque, 0 unbounded. **Ruled delta (INT ruling, PM concurrence, 2026-10-22, routed under Rule #271 clause 2):** the plan's `--check` "exits 1 on an export with no reader at all" is red on `main` today (234), and D4 forbids deleting in this wave, so `--check` is a ratchet against the tracked `test/dead-exports-baseline.json` (the 234, sorted, unique): red on a new dead export, a stale entry, a missing, malformed, duplicated or unsorted baseline, and graph uncertainty, each named; Stage 1 gains it as its `dead exports` step and reads green with `234 in the baseline, 0 new`. `test/audit-dead-exports.test.js` pins the classification and every red of the comparator. **Second ruled delta:** the plan named `bin/tc.js`, which does not exist; the roots are the three `bin/` commands, and `hooks/*` are shell and load no module. Stage 1 measured 2.2 s with the step (1.2 s of it the audit). Follow-up filed after a search of open issues: #2544, the first dead-export removal batch, citing the baseline; the test-only and orphan batches wait on W4-05's coverage matrix.

**Carried, and what was not touched.** From W4-02: the "under ten seconds" figures in `scripts/test-stage.js` and `test/test-stage.test.js` are gone (the time is printed, the PR carries it), and Stage 1's refusal compares `path.resolve(root)`. Not touched: `scripts/ci-evidence.js`, `scripts/suite-evidence.js` and their `ghRead` copies (the shared helper the W4-03 Critic noted is taken only if this surface is touched; it was not), `scripts/ci-complement.js`'s `decodeXml` citation (next touch), `test/test-tiers.test.js`'s helper-scan cleanup (W4-06). `.github/workflows/test.yml`, `.prawduct/project-state.yaml`, every gate and every rule are unchanged; #2371's activation stays Operator-held. Fragment `changelog.d/2498.w4-04-touched-graph.internal.md`; CHANGELOG and FEATURES entries in the PR body for the records PR; `test/tiers.json` lists the three new test files under `small`.

## 2026-10-22 — #2498 W4-03: the #2371 packet, `scripts/suite-evidence.js` undeclared and fail-closed

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2498-test-pyramid -->

#2498 W4-03. v5.35.0 Wave 4 chunk, plan r2 (W4-01 and W4-02 ticked at dispatch; checkpoint 4 decided); Refs #2498 and #2371, both open, #2362 untouched. Built on `feat/2498-w4-03-suite-evidence` from `origin/main` `1fb99f52` under the Integrator's W4-03 dispatch (queue rev 209, restart token `W4-03-20261022T1958Z-1FB99F52-A141A6`), the third chunk of the five-stage test pyramid and the one that carries #2371: the mechanism by which a green CI run on the exact head could be the suite evidence, written as a command the gate could be pointed at, and the written proposal of the exact cutover. Nothing in it changes what any gate accepts, what any gate runs, what CI does, or what any rule says.

**The command.** `node scripts/suite-evidence.js [--junit <path>] [--base <branch>] [--explain]`, declared nowhere. Its mode comes from the environment and from nothing typed: with `TANGLECLAW_SUITE_EVIDENCE` unset it hands node the declared `test_command` argument for argument (the list `scripts/test-stage.js` owns) and exits with the suite's code; with it set to `ci` it reads the ten facts of `scripts/ci-evidence.js` through that command's own exported step functions, in that command's order, then requires CI's own junit report as the run's `test-results` artifact (of this run and this commit, not expired, not empty, downloaded through `gh run download`, and the whole green report of one run by the identity `lib/test-results.js` pins), runs the complement with its own junit report, reads the tree and the ledger again, and writes the two reports merged into `<path>`: every suite of each verbatim, one more suite carrying the binding facts as `<property>` elements, one trailer summing the two runs plus that suite so the merged document obeys the same identity and reads as one green run. It fails closed: the first fact that does not hold, and an artifact that does not exist, which is the state of every run until the workflow step lands, is a named refusal, nothing is written, and the gate reads red; there is no synthetic report in any path. The artifact is listed before the complement runs, so today's refusal costs no wide run. Any other value of the variable refuses rather than picking a mode. `--explain` reads facts 1 to 8 and lists the artifact, says 9 and 10 need the run, prints the verdict, and runs nothing.

**What is pinned, and what is not touched.** `test/suite-evidence.test.js` (Small, every seam faked) pins the mode selection, local mode's argv byte-equal to the pinned `test_command` text and to this checkout's declaration where one exists, that the declaration still names `node --test` and the workflow still runs it and uploads no artifact (so the cutover cannot ride in unnoticed), each of the ten facts refusing alone and writing nothing, every way the artifact can be missing, the merge on fixtures of the shapes node 22's reporter produces (the `<undefined name="root">` wrapper included), `--explain` in both modes, and that `ci-evidence.js` is reused by import and keeps its own command (`test/ci-evidence.test.js` unchanged and green). `scripts/ci-evidence.js` itself is unchanged: its step functions were already exported. `.github/workflows/test.yml` and `.prawduct/project-state.yaml` are untouched; no rule was proposed through the API.

**The records.** `docs/lane-ci-evidence.md` gains the command's section (modes, the facts table, the merged report's properties, what it does not prove) and its PROPOSED, NOT IN FORCE section is rewritten against the mechanism: the order of the Operator-held steps (workflow step, rule texts at this page's digest, `test_command` line with its test pins, complement verified, one named pilot under a charter amendment, broader use decided from measurement), the Builder ring checkpoint 4 named (Stages 1+2+3, never Medium; no CI tier timing claimed; under-30 s to be measured with Stage 2), and the rule text naming `scripts/suite-evidence.js`, the stage runner and every full-suite exception the earlier text kept. Prawduct no longer has to change first, because the command reaches the gate as `test_command`; the draft report stays the Operator's to send and the page says the draft predates the mechanism. The activation packet, `.tangleclaw/plans/2371-activation-packet.md` (gitignored, hosted by the plans route, digest in the PR body), carries the exact cutover: the unified diff for `test.yml` (one `actions/upload-artifact` step pinned to the commit of `v7.0.2`, `if: always()`, `test-results.xml` as `test-results`, `git apply --check` green against `1fb99f52`), the `test_command` line, Rule #271 and Rule #282 as full replacement texts with only clause 4 and clauses 1/2(d) changed, the charter amendment request, the Operator's two asks, what the pilot would measure, and checkpoint 4 as decided. Fragment `changelog.d/2498.w4-03-suite-evidence.internal.md`; CHANGELOG and FEATURES entries in the PR body for the records PR; `test/tiers.json` lists the new test file under `small`.

## 2026-10-22 — #2498 W4-02: the stage runner, Stage 1, and the canonical suite summary

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2498-test-pyramid -->

#2498 W4-02. v5.35.0 Wave 4 chunk, plan r2 (W4-01 ticked at dispatch); Refs #2498 and #2371, both open. Built on `feat/2498-w4-02-stage-runner` from `origin/main` `f5c6ceb9` under the Integrator's W4-02 authorization (fresh-context token `W4-02-20261022T1759Z-F5C6CEB9`), the second chunk of the five-stage test pyramid: one entry point for the stages and the suite, and the one reader of what a run reported.

**The runner.** `node scripts/test-stage.js <1|2|3|4|5|all> [--junit <path>] [--spec <path|stdout>]`. Stage 1 is static and in-process where a check is a function: every tracked `.js` compiled by `vm.Script` in one process (a `node --check` per file would be a thousand spawns), `conflict-marker-scan`, `assert-strict-guard`, `sync-claude-md-block` in compare mode, `test-tiers --check`, and the three static-ring tests as one child; about a second on this host, each step timed and named. Stage 2 is not built (W4-04) and says so with a non-zero exit, because "ran nothing" must never read as green. Stages 3 and 4 run the tier's files from the ledger under its guard with `--test-timeout=300000`, the value measured at W4-01 and kept until re-measured; stage 5 runs the Large tier bare. `all` hands node the declared `test_command` argument for argument (`test/test-stage.test.js` pins the declared command as text and holds a checkout's declaration to it where one exists), so what the runner runs and what the gate records are one command; `--spec` appends the spec reporter only when asked, and stages 3 to 5 default it to stdout so a person sees the failing test. An empty tier runs nothing and says so: `node --test` with no files discovers its own, which would be the whole suite. Stages 3 + 4 + 5 are pinned equal to `test/*.test.js`.

**The reader, and the one plan delta.** The plan had `lib/test-results.js` refuse a report whose leaf count and summary "disagree by more than the file-level failures node reports as testcases", the exact rule to be pinned by a fixture. Measuring every shape node 22's junit reporter produces pinned an identity, not a tolerance: testcases + testsuites in the report equal tests + suites in the runner's summary. A `test()` with subtests is a test to the runner and a `<testsuite>` to the reporter; a `describe` whose body threw and a skipped `describe` are suites to the runner and `<testcase>`s to the reporter; each moves both sides by one, and the last full suite run obeys it (22,114 + 4,117 = 22,120 + 4,111). So `reconcile` refuses a report that breaks the identity, a report with no summary, and a summary whose outcomes do not sum to its `tests`; it reads red from the summary's `fail + cancelled`, and from a `<failure>` element the summary does not count, which is what a `describe` body that threw looks like: node reports `fail 0` and exits 0 for it, and the stage exits 1. The number the plan called `canonicalCount` is the reconciliation's `canonical`, the runner's `tests`: the canonical count is the summary's and never the leaf tally's, which is printed beside it. The junit reporter writes the summary as trailing comments (measured), so `summaryOf` reads a spec log, a TAP log or the report itself, and a run with `--spec <path>` is read from the spec log and pinned equal to the trailer.

**The move.** `parseJunit`, `decodeXml` and `attr` moved whole from `scripts/test-skip-audit.js` to `lib/test-results.js`; the audit re-exports `parseJunit`, so its readers are unchanged (`ci-complement.js` and `ci-evidence.js` import only the ledger functions, and `ci-complement.js` keeps its own narrower walker as before). `docs/areas.json` lists the new module under `tooling`, keeping the map total. `test/test-results.test.js` (Small) pins the tally, the three summary spellings, every measured shape and each refusal on fixtures; `test/test-stage.test.js` (Large) pins the argv contracts, Stage 1 green on this repository with every step named (its wall time is the pull request's measurement, not a contract a Large file can hold under `all`), and a real run on a fixture repository, through the real reporter, where stage 3 is green with a test with subtests and a describe skipped by option, and stage 5 and `all` are red on a describe that threw although node exited 0. Stage 1 is bound to this repository and says so: the `root` seam serves the test-running stages only, because the syntax step reads what git tracks here and the ledger check measures this repository's `test/` whatever ledger it is handed. `docs/test-pyramid.md` gains the stages and the reconciliation; `CONTRIBUTING.md` the stage commands; fragment `changelog.d/2498.w4-02-stage-runner.internal.md`; CHANGELOG and FEATURES entries in the PR body for the records PR. `test_command`, CI and every gate are unchanged; the tier timings are in the PR body, from the runs.

## 2026-10-22 — #2498 W4-01: the test tier ledger, its classifier and the Small/Medium guards

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2498-test-pyramid -->

#2498 W4-01. v5.35.0 Wave 4 chunk, plan r2 (sha256 `d2bd7814` at dispatch, no chunk ticked); Refs #2498 and #2371, both open. Built on `feat/2498-w4-01-tier-ledger` from `origin/main` `598ab72e` under the Integrator's W4-01 authorization (queue revision 181), the first chunk of the five-stage test pyramid: the declaration of which tier each test file is in, the tool that proposes and checks it, and the guards that make the tiers true at run time.

**What a tier is, and the one plan delta.** `test/tiers.json` lists every `test/*.test.js` exactly once under `small` (starts no subprocess, opens no `node:sqlite` database, listens on no socket), `medium` (starts no subprocess) or `large` (anything). The plan wrote the guards as refusing the *require*; they refuse the *act*. The first guarded run showed why: a hundred-odd files that never spawn load a product module (`lib/exec.js`, `lib/caddy.js`, `lib/store/_db.js`) that requires `child_process` or `node:sqlite` at its top, and refusing the load would have made them Large for nothing they do. So `test/helpers/preload-small.js` hands back `child_process` with every spawner replaced by a refusal and `node:sqlite` with a refusing `DatabaseSync`, and makes `net.Server.prototype.listen` refuse; `test/helpers/preload-medium.js` does the first of those only. A refusal names the act, the calling line and the test file, is announced on stderr as well as thrown, and fails the file even when product code catches the throw and degrades (a failed `git remote` read as "no origin" would otherwise let a test pass for the wrong reason): the guard counts refusals and exits the file's process non-zero, which the runner reports as the file failing. Routed to the Integrator as an unplanned choice before the suite, with both measurements.

**What the guarded runs found.** Under the Medium guard most server-booting tests made a refused spawn, nearly all from `lib/tmux.js`'s `execSync` probes, `lib/engines.js`'s engine-CLI probes and `lib/dir-scanner.js`'s `fork`: a test that boots the server is Large today because the boot path spawns, not because it tests a subprocess, and the ledger records that rather than hiding it; making those probes injectable is product work for a later chunk. One such file waited forever after the refusal instead of failing, so a guarded tier is run with `--test-timeout`, which the page says and the stage runner (next chunk) will carry.

**The classifier and the contract.** `scripts/test-tiers.js --propose` names the fastest tier a file's text allows, following its requires of other files under `test/` (a helper that spawns makes its callers Large) and never into `lib/`; a spawner call counts only in a file that also binds `child_process`, because the first rule read `re.exec(` and SQLite's `db.exec(` as spawns and over-pinned 82 files as Large until the Critic's review named it, after which the ledger was regenerated and every demotion re-earned by the guarded runs; `--write` regenerates the ledger from the scan and keeps every declaration slower than the scan allows, which is how a file the guard refused stays demoted; `--check` refuses a ledger that is not total (a file missing, listed twice or gone) or that declares a file faster than its text allows, naming each failure; `--list <tier>` prints a tier's files for the runner. The ledger's first contents are the scan corrected by the guarded runs; the counts are `--check`'s output, and a tier's time is its guarded run's, both in the pull request rather than here. `test/test-tiers.test.js` pins the scan on fixtures, the ledger's totality and honesty, the tracked ledger itself, and each guard's refusals in a real `node --test` child, the swallowed one included. `docs/test-pyramid.md` is the page; fragment `changelog.d/2498.w4-01-tier-ledger.internal.md`; CHANGELOG and FEATURES entries in the PR body for the records PR; the stage runner, `test_command`, CI and every gate are unchanged.

**What moved, unchanged.** `lib/routes/tc.js` holds the family's twenty-four routes inside a `register(deps)` called where the family's first route stood — the launch sequence (`POST start/next`, `POST start/ready`, `GET start/status`, `GET start/review`) and the rules-delivery receipt (`POST rule-receipt`); the lane's own workload (`POST`/`GET workload`) and the operator's narrowing (`POST workload/narrowing`); the governed coordinator rotation (`rotation/prepare`, `GET rotation`, `advance`, `resume`, `relaunch`, `abandon`) with the operator's reads that share its helpers (`GET /api/rotations`, `GET`/`POST /api/coordinator-roles`, `POST /api/coordinator-roles/revoke`); `GET whoami` and `GET sessions`; and the four check watches (`POST`/`GET check-watches`, `GET check-watches/:id`, `POST check-watches/:id/cancel`, #2323, which postdate the plan and were ruled in by the Integrator as a scope delta so that no tc route remains in the entry point) — and at module level in the order they held: `_launchIdentity`, `_rotationAccess`, the capability rows (`_provenanceCapability`, `_startupControlCapability`), the binding verdict and awareness receipt (`_admittedAs`, `_whoamiBinding`, `awarenessVerbLabel`, `resolveClaimedProject`, `writeAwarenessReceipt`, `recordTcAwareness`), the lane composition (`_composedLane`, `_laneRotation`) and the check-watch wrapper (`checkWatchRoute`). The family sat in three regions of `server.js` around the operator bridge's HTTP surface and the merge-queue routes, which stay; the single register call puts the rotation routes ahead of the bridge's and the check watches at the family's end, a route-order change across non-overlapping families that the reachability test accepts, with the route set and every registration option identical. The coordinator epoch gate (`coordinatorGateRefused`) and `_engineThread`, which it asks, moved whole to `lib/routes/_callers.js` beside the caller proofs, because the session-rule, Medusa, control, wrap and session-command mutations all apply the one gate; `server.js` takes it from the callers module on its existing destructure, so its fifteen call sites and the two `deps` injections into the wrap and session families are unchanged lines. The callers module now requires `control-auth` and `coordinator-rotation` eagerly, which brings the store into a module that so far held only the gate and access readers; it changes no load, since the entry point loaded all of it before. `register(deps)` receives the one server-local value the family reads, the activity observer, held in the module for the lane composition, which the entry point reads back for the bridge's lane reader beside the dispatcher's admission fields and awareness receipt. Four requires whose only readers moved left `server.js` (`./lib/workload`, `./lib/workload-fleet`, `./lib/ecosystem-primer`'s `workloadSentence`, `./lib/check-watch-api`).

**What stayed, and the map.** `_startupControlForLaunch`, which the plan listed with this family, stays in `server.js` with its only reader, `GET /api/launch-sequences` (the Integrator recorded the plan correction). The dispatcher's awareness interception, which skips `whoami` by name and records every other tc verb through the imported `recordTcAwareness`, is dispatcher plumbing and stays. `tc` leaves `docs/areas.json`'s `shared.holds` and the `tc` area owns the new file beside `lib/tc-verbs.js`.

**Text readers and records.** No test pins moved text: the plan's two named readers (`test/tracked-carrier-identity.test.js`, `test/api-medusa.test.js`) grep `server.js` for shared-docs routes and boot order, both outside the regions, and the step-0 literal scan of the three regions against all 46 `server.js` text readers ran before the first narrow run. **What moved, unchanged.** The `// ── Launch sequences (Train 21) ──` section left `lib/store.js` as two files, because the step-0 matrix showed its two halves share nothing. `lib/store/launch.js` holds the first half (`LAUNCH_STEP_IDS`, `_insertLaunchSequence`, `_claimInheritedNotice`, `projectRecoveryStateApi`, `recoveryClearancesApi`, `_insertRecoveryClearBatchItem`, `recoveryClearBatchesApi`, `_clearRecoveryCas`, `OPERATOR_RECOVERY_CLEARANCES`, `launchSequencesApi`, and the six `_rowTo*` mappers they read through). `lib/store/handoffs.js` holds the second (`_rowToHandoffPublication`, `_rowToHandoffEpoch`, `handoffEpochApi`, `handoffsApi`) together with the `EPOCH_ROW_MISSING` constant from the facade's header, whose only reader is the epoch api. Three regions cut by line range and emitted byte for byte; the facade imports back the eight names it still exports and re-exports them under the names it always exported. The `HANDOFF_*`, `EPOCH_UNKNOWN_FROM_V41` and `RECOVERY_BATCH_OUTCOMES` constants the plan listed already lived in `lib/store/_schema.js` since T01 and did not move again.

**The seam (INT `83a1e7c7`, Architect A410).** Each module keeps its own `let _db` and guards over it with its own `_ensureDb`. The handoffs module needs nothing but the handle and the schema's baseline vocabulary, which it requires from `./_schema` directly (the schema requires no domain module), so it is handle-only in `BOUND_MODULES`. The launch module filters two readers on `SESSION_STATUS.ACTIVE`, a constant the sessions domain owns, while `lib/store/sessions.js` requires the launch module for the insert its `start()` runs: requiring sessions back would be a cycle, so the constant arrives beside the handle, `launch._bind({ db, SESSION_STATUS })` from `init()` and `_bind(null)` from `close()`, the way the schema already receives the same constant. `lib/store/sessions.js` is the one re-pointed reader: it now requires `./launch` and `./handoffs` directly, drops the two injected `let`s, and its `_bind(db)` shrinks to the handle as it joins `BOUND_MODULES`, still clearing the launch-blocker warning memory when unbound. Dependency graph: `_db ← _schema ← handoffs`; `_db ← launch`; `sessions → launch, handoffs`; the facade requires all of them; nothing under `lib/store/` requires the facade.

**Records and tests.** `docs/areas.json`: the `launch` area gains both files; `shared.holds` keeps `launch` because its routes are still in `server.js`. Two lifecycle pins (PM `ab65826c`): `test/handoff-store.test.js` and `test/launch-sequence.test.js` each close the store, assert the moved apis refuse with `NOT_INITIALIZED`, re-open it and assert they answer again. No `docs/*`, `FEATURES.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md` line names a moved symbol, so none is repointed and `FEATURES.md` is untouched. `changelog.d/2248.t04-store-launch.internal.md` carries the `### Internal` entry.

## 2026-10-22 — #2248 T01: the store handle, the schema and the activity log out of lib/store.js

<!-- prawduct: type=chore | scope=2248-area-extraction -->

#2248 T01. v5.35.0 Wave 3 chunk W3-A1, plan r2 (sha256 `7cde3dbc` at dispatch; accepted by INT with Architect A404 and PM rulings `2854e406`/`d6346a6a`); Part of #2248, which stays open for Part 2. Built on `feat/2248-t01-store-keystone` from `origin/main` `f480a783` under dispatch token `W3-T01-2248-F480A783-7CDE3DBC`.

**What moved, unchanged.** `lib/store/_db.js` holds the error class, the initialization guard (`_ensureDb`), the JSON reader, `getDb`, and the two transaction wrappers (`_withSavepoint`, `_startupControlTransaction`); `lib/store/_schema.js` holds `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`, the vocabulary lists the CHECK constraints are built from (the startup-fire, recovery-batch, workload-nudge, handoff-baseline and lease lists with their `_SQL` forms), `_createTables`, `_runMigrations` and every migration, postcondition and per-area DDL helper the chain calls, including `_migrateHandoffEpoch` with its four helpers, `_migrateLaunchRecovery` and `_migrateDropMethodology`; `lib/store/activity.js` holds the `// ── Activity Log ──` block and `_rowToActivity`. Fourteen blocks, cut by line range and emitted byte for byte; the three lazy `require` paths inside the chain climb one directory, and nothing else in a moved line changed.

**The seam.** Each module keeps its own `let _db` and a `_bind`; the facade binds the handle module, the schema and every entry of `BOUND_MODULES` right after it opens the database and unbinds them in `close()`, so every `_db.` call site reads as it did. A domain module guards over its own handle (its own `_ensureDb`, same text as the shared one), not the handle module's: that is what makes a module the facade forgot to bind fail at its first strict use with `NOT_INITIALIZED` instead of passing the shared guard and dropping events silently (Critic R-1). The schema receives what it calls into by injection, `_bind({ db, dbFile, projectConfigApi, SESSION_STATUS })`, and never requires the facade (A404); the step-0 scan found two injected values beyond the api the plan named: `_dbFile` (the pre-v28 backup path) and `SESSION_STATUS` (one read in `_classifyHandoffBaseline`). `_startupControlTransaction` moved to the handle module rather than staying in the facade because the chain calls it eighteen times and it reads nothing but the handle. The facade imports back the 26 names the domains still read and re-exports them under the names it always exported; its `merge-queue-state` and `check-watch-state` requires lost their last reader and are removed.

**Text readers and tests.** No test grepping `lib/store.js` lost its needle. `test/self-improvement-loop.test.js` evicted the facade from the require cache for a fresh store instance; with the handle in its own module that left the suite's store sharing one handle module with the fresh one, so it now evicts the whole `lib/store/` family. `test/tc-require-graph.test.js` names the directory too. New pins: the schema source requires no facade and the facade binds it before the chain runs; every `lib/store/*.js` whose exports name `_bind` is required by the facade, bound in `init()`'s body by name or through `BOUND_MODULES`, and (for a domain module) guards its own handle; a pre-v28 database with a `minimal` project is seeded through the injected api and loses the column; the activity domain refuses with `NOT_INITIALIZED` after `close()` and answers again after the next `init()`.

**Map and docs.** `docs/areas.json` gains `store` (the handle and the schema) and `activity`; the three `docs/configuration-reference.md` lines that locate the DDL and the schema version point at `lib/store/_schema.js`, and the four `FEATURES.md` anchors naming moved symbols point at their new files, under the PM's bounded-anchor ruling.

## 2026-10-21 — #1220 C04: the repository checks its own fragments, the PR gate is recorded as a proposal, and features-toc never stubs a fragment (#2519)

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=1220-changelog-fragments -->

#1220 C04. v5.35.0 Wave 2 chunk W2-A1, plan r5 (C01 merged as `81891ab3`, C02 as `c774d21d`, C03 as `bdd8b15f`; Architect A391.3 ruled the PR gate an Operator-gated proposal; the PM's scope ruling relayed by the Integrator adds `lib/wrap-steps/_source-paths.js` and `test/wrap-step-features-toc.test.js` so this chunk closes #2519), restart token `W2-1220-C04-BDD8B15F-20261021-7D3C`. Branch `feat/1220-c04-changelog-fragments` from `origin/main` `bdd8b15f`.

**Step 0, as the plan asks.** C04's release read from `LANE_QUEUE` rev 67 (Batch State `WAVE_2_C04_RELEASED`). The open pull-request list taken with `gh pr list --state open --json number,createdAt`: #2385 (created 2026-10-13T17:07:57Z) and #2217 (created 2026-10-08T00:43:51Z), both before the Q4 boundary (`c774d21d`, 2026-10-21T18:39:01Z). So the transition wording in the three docs, the `changelog-update` prompt's pre-boundary step and the direct-edit coverage route all stay this chunk, as the plan's "Done when" says; their removal is #2523, filed from this chunk, and the pull request body says so. `lib/wrap-default-pipeline.js`, `lib/wrap-steps/changelog-coverage.js` and `test/wrap-changelog-coverage.test.js` are therefore not edited.

**What changed.** New `test/changelog-fragments-repo.test.js`: `readFragments(REPO_ROOT)` on every test run, failing on any refusal with the file and its code, passing on an empty or missing directory; fixture cases pin both passing states and that a stray `README.md` and a heading-bodied fragment are each named, so the check is not vacuous. CI runs `test/*.test.js`, so this is the fail-closed schema check with no change to `.github/workflows/test.yml`. `docs/release-process.md` gains "Proposed: PR fragment gate (NOT IN FORCE)" carrying the gate C05 would add word for word: which pull requests (a diff under `lib/`, `server.js`, `public/` or `scripts/`), what satisfies it (a name `parseFragmentName` accepts), the `Changelog: none <reason>` escape, fail-closed on unreadable inputs, exit codes 0/1/4, the one `pull_request`-only workflow step, the three things that must happen before it is in force, and that the transition exception ends separately when #2385 and #2217 close. `docs/changelog-fragments.md` and `CONTRIBUTING.md` carry the same heading as a pointer to that one text, so the proposal has one carrier. `lib/wrap-steps/_source-paths.js`: `isSourceFile` refuses the fragment directory by its own constant, `FRAGMENT_DIR_PREFIX`, with the reason inline (a fragment is the log, not work to log or index; coverage names a stray there by its own rule, so nothing weakens). It is deliberately not in `EXCLUDED_PREFIXES`: `features-toc` reads that list to skip tokens when it reports dangling `FEATURES.md` citations, and a cited fragment is exactly the citation that dangles once the release deletes the file, which that report exists to say (the Critic's second warning on the first head, which had put it in the prefix list). `test/wrap-step-source-paths.test.js` pins the constant, that it is not a prefix exclusion, that only the root directory is excluded, and the no-drift list; `test/wrap-step-features-toc.test.js` pins `_isIndexableCandidate` on a fragment, at the handler that a session whose only touched file is a fragment is skipped with `FEATURES.md` untouched, and that a dangling fragment citation is still reported; the first two fail with the exclusion removed and pass with it, run both ways. Two fragments, one per issue as `CONTRIBUTING.md` asks: `changelog.d/1220.c04-fragment-obligation.changed.md` and `changelog.d/2519.features-toc-never-stubs-fragments.fixed.md`. `CHANGELOG.md` and `FEATURES.md` are untouched; their entries are in the pull request body for the Integrator's records PR.

**Decisions the plan text did not settle.** The #2519 fix is a classifier exclusion rather than a filter in `features-toc`'s `_isIndexableCandidate`, because the classifier is the one place both callers read; it is a check of its own inside `isSourceFile` rather than a prefix-list entry, because the prefix list has a second reader in `features-toc` (the dangling-citation report) that must keep seeing the directory. A second fragment for #2519 rather than one fragment naming both issues, because the contributor contract says work on several issues gets one fragment each. The pilot branches (`1220.pilot-a.added.md`, `1220.pilot-b.fixed.md`, the colliding throwaway) are asked of the Integrator in the ready report, as the plan says, and the evidence lands in the pull request body when they merge.

## 2026-10-21 — #1220 C03: a release cut compiles the fragments and stays recoverable until it lands

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=1220-changelog-fragments -->

#1220 C03. v5.35.0 Wave 2 chunk W2-A1, plan r5 (C01 merged as `81891ab3`, C02 as `c774d21d`; Architect A391-R4 approved the C03 design with Safeguards 1, 2 and 3(a)–(e), A394 ruled stray files fail closed), restart token `W2-1220-C03-C774D21D-20261021-4E7A`. Branch `feat/1220-c03-changelog-fragments` from `origin/main` `c774d21d`.

**What changed.** `lib/wrap-steps/version-bump.js`: before `[Unreleased]` is parsed, every fragment under `changelog.d/` is read and spliced into the block in memory, so the bump level, the readiness gate and the promotion read one text; each compiled fragment is staged for deletion (`{primingPath, newContent:null, delete:true, fragment:true}`) beside the promoted `CHANGELOG.md`, and `output.fragments` records what was compiled. Two new halts with `needs-operator` and their own remediation, neither carrying `wouldBump` so the drawer offers no Cut/Hold: a file the fragment contract refuses (named with its code; a skip would promote the block without it), and a leftover `.tangleclaw/release-recovery/<run id>/manifest.json` under the config root (checked before any version or CHANGELOG file is read). `lib/wrap-steps/commit.js`: the flush and the snapshot accept the delete shape, and a target already gone is an error (Safeguard 1); `_realpathOr` resolves a missing path through its real parent so a deleted fragment still matches the realpath toplevel and lands in the commit (Safeguard 2); every release cut, with or without a `releasePrepareCommand`, snapshots its targets' prior bytes and the `git ls-files -s` entries of exactly those targets before the flush, writes both to the recovery record (one file per target plus `manifest.json`, in a directory that ignores itself), and every halt between the flush and a landed commit restores the work tree, and the index entries once `git add` ran, through one builder that says what was put back and what was not; a commit stopped by its timeout restores nothing and names the record; the record is removed after a landed commit or a complete restore and retained otherwise (Safeguard 3(a)–(e)). A release cut commits all of its files or none: a release file the operator left out of the commit, whether every one or only `CHANGELOG.md` beside an included bump and fragment deletion, refuses the commit before anything is branched or staged, puts the cut back and asks for every release file or a Hold (the Critic's R-2: a half cut would consume fragments never published, with nothing refusing the next cut). A path this wrap is authorized to commit that was added to the index this session and then deleted by the flush (porcelain `AD` intersected with the stageable selection: a fragment the session had `git add`-ed before the wrap; never an operator's own left-out or foreign `AD` file, whose real-index entry the untrack route's sync must not touch, the Integrator's finding on the first amendment) stays in the `git add` selection, which removes its index entry, and leaves the commit pathspec, which would otherwise fail on a path known to neither HEAD, the index nor the work tree before any hook ran (Architect A397); the untrack route leaves it out of its temporary-index add and brings the real index level after the commit; a wrap whose only selected paths are such entries skips with nothing to commit, and a release cut in that state (unreachable: its CHANGELOG is always selected) is put back. The commit body gains `- Compiled N changelog fragment(s)` beside the `Bumped` line. `lib/release-readiness.js`: pending fragments pass `unreleased-entries`, a refused set is `unknown`. `lib/wrap-steps/_release-recommendation.js`: the decision is open on pending fragments under an `[Unreleased]` heading, closed without the heading (version-bump would skip) and closed on a refused set. `scripts/release-prepare.js`: refuses while `changelog.d/` still holds a valid fragment. Tests: new `test/version-bump-fragments.test.js` (compile equals hand-written, coexistence, second-run no-op, staged deletions, hold stages nothing, the leftover record, the one spelling of the directory); the refused-fragment halt in `test/version-bump-fail-closed.test.js`; twenty-one real-repository cases in `test/wrap-changelog-transaction.test.js` (the one-commit cut, the release-prepare failure, a failing `git add`, a rejected and a timed-out `git commit`, an untracked fragment whose restore fails, an unrelated edit surviving, pre-existing partial staging put back byte for byte under a failing `git add` and a failing pre-commit hook (the hook's own refusal asserted, A397), a pre-staged new fragment consumed by a successful cut with its bullet under the release heading and the file absent from HEAD, index and work tree, an added-then-deleted-only wrap skipping, the untrack route beside an added-then-deleted path, an unselected foreign `AD` path keeping its real-index entry byte-identical through an approved un-track, a failed index restore, a failing pre-add index read, the all-or-nothing rule with every release file left out and with only `CHANGELOG.md` left out, the symlinked tmpdir); the readiness, recommendation, release-prepare and unreleased-size cases. Docs: `docs/changelog-fragments.md` loses its "not yet wired" note and gains "When compilation happens" in full; `docs/release-process.md` names compilation in the chain, the C02 merge SHA and time on the transition boundary, and the recovery case under "If a release did not go out"; `docs/configuration-reference.md` names the record. New `changelog.d/1220.c03-release-compiles-fragments.changed.md`. `CHANGELOG.md` and `FEATURES.md` are untouched; their entries are in the pull request body for the Integrator's records PR.

**Decisions the plan text did not settle, each narrower than the plan or inside it.** The record directory ignores itself with a `.gitignore` holding `*`, because `_tc-owned-paths.js` names TangleClaw state file by file and is outside this chunk's files; without it a project that tracks `.tangleclaw/` would be asked about the record at every cut. An unmerged index entry (stage other than 0) on a release target refuses the cut before the flush, since `--cacheinfo` can only put stage 0 back. `lib/wrap-steps/preflight.js` was read and not edited: it has no summary of its own to name the record in, and `version-bump`'s refusal is the surface an operator meets first. `_release-recommendation.js` closes the decision on a refused set rather than reading it as open, because `version-bump` stops on that set before any recommendation could matter.

## 2026-10-21 — #1220 C02: the wrap asks for a changelog fragment and accepts one

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=1220-changelog-fragments -->

#1220 C02. v5.35.0 Wave 2 chunk W2-A1, plan r5 (C01 merged as `81891ab3`; Architect A394 settled the C01 contract C02 builds on), restart token `W2-1220-C02-81891AB3-20261021-9C4E`. Branch `feat/1220-c02-changelog-fragments` from `origin/main` `81891ab3`.

**What changed.** `lib/wrap-default-pipeline.js`: the `changelog-update` step declares `changelog.d/` as a second produced path beside `CHANGELOG.md`, and its prompt asks the session's AI for one fragment per issue, `changelog.d/<issue>.<slug>.<type>.md`, with the name grammar, the seven types and the bump each implies, the one-list-entry body rule, and `CHANGELOG.md` named as accepted only for a branch whose pull request predates the project's move to fragments; `version-bump`'s order note now covers the fragments too. `lib/wrap-steps/changelog-coverage.js`: a path directly under `changelog.d/` whose name `parseFragmentName` accepts counts as the changelog being maintained, by the dirty route and the committed-history route alike; a misnamed, nested or stray file there (`README.md` included, which the shared source classifier would call bookkeeping) counts for nothing and is named as unlogged work by the gate's own rule, so it fails closed on it. No `coveragePaths` glob is declared for the directory, because a glob would accept the misnamed file (step 0's finding: the step spec is read for `coveragePaths`, but the glob is the wrong tool). `lib/wrap-steps/ai-content.js`: the default verify reader digests a declared directory (sorted direct entries with kind, size and mtime) instead of reading it as `null`, so a fragment the AI creates is a change to the mutation check and to the file-settle completion signal, which read through the same function; for `changelog.d/` only entries the fragment contract names are listed, so a misnamed file written during the step reads as no change and the predicate names it (the Critic's one substantive finding: the mutation route runs first and would otherwise have passed it); a plain file still reads as its content. Tests: thirteen coverage cases for fragments and the `_isFragmentPath` contract pin, five pipeline-spec cases for the declaration and the prompt, seven reader cases on a real temporary tree including two `run()` end to end with the real reader and real git. Docs: `docs/changelog-fragments.md` gains "How a wrap produces one" and says what the wrap now calls; `docs/release-process.md` "What is still manual" gains the fragment paragraph and the transition boundary; `CONTRIBUTING.md` step 4 becomes "add a fragment", with the subsection table re-keyed by type and the pre-boundary exception. New `changelog.d/1220.c02-wrap-produces-fragments.changed.md`, the first fragment in the repo, written under the contract it enables. `CHANGELOG.md` and `FEATURES.md` are untouched; their entries are in the pull request body for the Integrator's records PR.

**Outside this chunk, filed as #2519.** `features-toc` would stub a fragment as a source file on a project with the feature index on. The fix is a `changelog.d/` exclusion in `lib/wrap-steps/_source-paths.js` or in `features-toc`'s own candidate filter; neither file is in C02's list, and the plan reserved the classifier, so it is filed rather than done.

## 2026-10-21 — #1220 C01: the changelog fragment contract, parser and compiler

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=1220-changelog-fragments -->

#1220 C01. v5.35.0 Wave 2 chunk W2-A1, plan r5 (Architect A391-R4, Integrator release of C01 only), restart token `W2-1220-C01-R31-20261021-5D8A`. Branch `feat/1220-c01-changelog-fragments` from `origin/main` `cf872d39`.

**What changed.** New `lib/changelog-fragments.js`: the closed contract for `changelog.d/<issue>.<slug>.<type>.md`, a reader that enumerates and validates the directory and fails closed on any refusal (`MALFORMED_NAME`, `BAD_ISSUE`, `BAD_SLUG`, `UNKNOWN_TYPE`, `NOT_A_FILE`, `SYMLINK`, `PATH_ESCAPE`, `NOT_A_DIRECTORY`, `EMPTY_BODY`, `BAD_BODY`), a compiler that groups accepted fragments under the seven Keep a Changelog headings in subsection order with entries sorted by issue, then slug, then type so enumeration order never reaches the output, and a pure splice that writes the sections into `[Unreleased]` and returns every byte outside that block unchanged. No bump derivation and no require of `version-bump`: the module's block-finding regexes are its own constants, pinned equal to `version-bump`'s by the test, which reads both. Production requires are `node:fs`, `node:path` and `./logger` only; refusals log at `warn`, the happy path logs nothing. New `test/changelog-fragments.test.js` (48 cases): every refusal code by name, every type under its heading, identical output under reversed and shuffled enumeration, a missing directory as nothing pending, a symlinked directory refused, splice positions before, between and after existing subsections, a fence inside `[Unreleased]` left alone, non-idempotence named, released sections byte-identical, the regex pin, and the bump-level fixtures (`BREAKING:`, `BREAKING(`, `NOT BREAKING —`, minor-only, patch-only) judged by `version-bump._decideBumpLevel` on the spliced text. New `docs/changelog-fragments.md`: the contract as a contributor reads it, marked as not yet wired into the wrap until C03.

**Three departures from the plan text, all narrower than it, each written into the plan's C01 section after the Critic round.** The logger require from `lib/` is `./logger`, not the plan's `../logger` (copied from `lib/wrap-steps/`). The issue number must have no leading zero, so each issue has one canonical name (`NAME_RE` and the parser agree, and the test holds them to the same verdict). `NOT_A_DIRECTORY` is one code the plan did not list: it is what a file handed in as the root (`.prawduct/change-log.md` included) or a non-directory `changelog.d` gets, so that read path is a refusal and never a read. No `changelog.d/` is created; it first appears with C02's own fragment. `CHANGELOG.md` and `FEATURES.md` are untouched; their entries are in the pull request body for the Integrator's records PR.

## 2026-10-19 — #2370: a listener that reconnects after a Hub outage keeps its registered connection

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w23b1-2370-listener-stale-reconnect -->

v5.34.0 Wave 23 chunk W23-B1, the one chunk of #2370. Branch `fix/w23b1-2370-listener-stale-reconnect` from `origin/main` `9605ac2b`. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w23b1-2370-listener-stale-reconnect.md` (Rev1 sha256 `7453bdf1…bc44c43`; INT approved, Architect A363). Charter v2.57 (sha256 `d0a1e1a0…22b7`, 3-file lock, A362).

**What changed.** After a Hub outage the listener reconnected and registered, then closed that working connection one backoff step later and registered again. Two causes, both fixed in `lib/medusa-listener.js` (root cause from Architect A362):
- Node's built-in `WebSocket` reports closing a socket that is still connecting as an `error` dispatched synchronously inside `close()` ("Connection was closed before it was established", measured on Node 22.22.3). The listener closed superseded sockets while they were still `_ws`, so that error passed the identity guard and armed a fresh reconnect. All three close sites (`_connect`, the handshake deadline, `stop`) now detach the socket before closing it. The deadline's `HANDSHAKE_TIMEOUT` code is no longer overwritten by `CONNECT_FAILED`.
- `registered` now cancels any pending reconnect, so no reconnect armed by an earlier failure can replace a registered socket.

**Evidence.** Four tests in `test/medusa-listener.test.js` use a fake socket that closes as Node 22 was measured to, and drive time with `mock.timers`. Each was seen red on the unfixed code. Reverting each fix hunk alone reds only its own test, and the reconnect test stays green with only the `registered` change reverted. No live Hub was touched: the fix is proved against the fake, not by restarting a Hub. Approach credit: @CRYPTONIKAV's comment on #2370 proposed the same `registered` change (item ii); the external fork was not opened.

## 2026-10-19 — #2240 Chunk 02: each soak switchboard call is made by its session, inside its own pane

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=w22-a1-soak-session-identity -->

v5.34.0 Wave 22 chunk W22-A1, chunk 2 of 2 of #2240 (item 1, and the expected criteria still open). Closes #2240. Branch `fix/2240-w22-a1-session-identity` from `origin/main` `2cf701d5`. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/w22-a1-2240-chunk02-session-identity.md` (Rev3 sha256 `bf422d93…9e58`, INT-approved; Architect A357 rulings D1–D5, A358 stamp). Charter v2.56 (sha256 `7b6a6a1e…52f3`, 19-file lock, A356).

**What changed.** Under the #2233 launch-binding floor, the driver's own `medusa/send` and `medusa/read` were refused, so `engine.session.medusa-cycle` always failed at `send`; and the driver's inbox poll was recorded as a read by someone other than the recipient.
- The stub engine now handles `/soak-medusa send|read` lines by making the switchboard call itself, with its pane's `TANGLECLAW_LAUNCH_ID` and `TANGLECLAW_PROJECT_ID` only: no cookie, CSRF or bearer token, `TANGLECLAW_API`'s origin only, no redirect followed. Before any call it asks `whoami` (as a side lookup) and goes on only when the binding is `verified` and the project and workspace are its own and the route's, since the server does not compare a valid binding with the route's project (#1875). It prints one short nonce-tagged result line, with no launch id, project id or message body, and finishes each command within one 50-second deadline, below the driver's 60-second wait for that line.
- The executor types those lines into each pane as the Operator (`/command`) and reads the result back through `peek`. The sender's session sends; the recipient's session reads its own inbox and marks the message handled. The driver holds no launch id and makes no switchboard write or inbox read of its own.
- A server refusal reaches the outcome with its own code (`LAUNCH_BINDING_REQUIRED`, `LAUNCH_BINDING_INVALID`, …). New harness codes: `IN_PANE_SILENT`, `IN_PANE_NO_API`, `IN_PANE_NO_BINDING`, `IN_PANE_BINDING_UNVERIFIED`, `IN_PANE_WRONG_PROJECT`, `IN_PANE_WHOAMI_FAILED`.
- The principal table gains the `session` principal: the medusa cycle's `/command` is the Operator's, and its send and read are each session's own.
- Chunk 01's riders (Architect A353; observation O-1 of `rev-20261019T035934Z-17ad6123`): the README says a browser event that lands on the sign-in page (`LOGIN_SHOWN`) drops the Operator session, so a browser-only schedule can end in `REAUTH_EXHAUSTED`; the run runbook's step 5 no longer sends a gate-open guest that still has its login file to be recreated, since re-running setup finishes it.

**Evidence.** Unit tests drive the stub through in-memory streams and the executor through a fake fleet whose panes run the real stub client against a fake server with the binding floor; they pin every outcome code, the whoami refusal set, the project-mismatch refusal (`LAUNCH_BINDING_INVALID`, A357's unit denial), that every switchboard call carries only its own session's binding, and that no launch id reaches an outcome. A host compatibility run (D4, Architect A357) on an isolated scratch server, at the head the PR names: temp `HOME` and `TANGLECLAW_HOME`, a private tmux server (`TMUX_TMPDIR` in the temp home, `TMUX` unset), `TANGLECLAW_TTYD_WATCHER=off`, this lane's launch variables stripped, PortHub-leased loopback ports 5221–5224 released afterwards. One `engine.session.medusa-cycle` ended `OK`, the exchange record naming soak-a's session as the verified sender (proof `launch`) and the recipient as reader and acknowledger (proof `launch`). Denials end to end: no binding `LAUNCH_BINDING_REQUIRED`, an unknown launch id `LAUNCH_BINDING_INVALID`, a send typed into the recipient's pane naming the sender's route refused in the pane with `WRONG_PROJECT` and no exchange row. No launch id, password or session cookie in the soak's records. The default tmux socket's session list was identical before and after. Owed to the next Tart guest dry run (A344 D8, owner TC-Lane-A): the engine, fault and Safari cycles in a real guest, A3 (Add Cookie with `httpOnly`), and guest-setup steps 5 and 6 with Caddy.

## 2026-10-19 — #2240 Chunk 01: the soak signs in as an Operator account, with CSRF and a bounded 401 policy

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=w21-a1-soak-operator-auth -->

v5.34.0 Wave 21 chunk W21-A1, chunk 1 of 2 of #2240 (items 2, 3 and 4; item 1 is Chunk 02, which closes it). Refs #2240. Branch `fix/2240-w21-a1-soak-operator-auth` from `origin/main` `7641eb92`. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/w21-a1-2240-chunk01-soak-operator-auth.md` (rev1 sha256 `b1695551…ea9c`, Architect A344 D1-D8, A345). Charter v2.55 (sha256 `a93ea15c…7c86`, 18-file lock, A348). Rulings during the build: A346 (secrets only in files, never on stdout or a command line) and A351 (the three-step first run below, after the cumulative Critic found that setup with an account starts the Caddy cutover on a guest that has Caddy).

**What changed.** The #2233 launch-binding floor refuses the soak's launches, kills, commands, wraps, restarts and leases from anyone but the Operator, a Master or a live session, and the service token stands for nobody outside the port routes. So the soak now signs in as an account.
- `guest-setup.sh` step 5 creates a `soak-operator` login as the workload user, a `0600` file the new `soak.js operator-login` writes. A first run then finishes setup with no login, turns the login on (`add-login`) and creates the account (`set-password`), so no Caddy cutover starts; the dashboard header goes only on the two calls made while the gate is open. A re-run reads the gate and does only what is missing: it finishes an interrupted first run, signs in to an armed guest with the file, or refuses with "recreate the guest". Every later write, step 6's hub leases included, goes with the session and CSRF. Secret-bearing calls run as the workload user, with each secret only in its own `mktemp` files.
- `executors.call(..., { as: 'operator' })` carries the session on a closed list of writes, to the target's own origin only. Reads and `medusa/send`/`read` carry none: those stay failing closed until Chunk 02 carries each session's binding.
- `soak.js run --operator-login` signs in by gate state before any load (`OPERATOR_LOGIN_UNAVAILABLE` / `OPERATOR_LOGIN_FAILED` otherwise). A 401 is never retried (`UNAUTHENTICATED`). The next Operator event signs in once; a failure is `REAUTH_FAILED`, and three in a row stop the run cleanly (`REAUTH_EXHAUSTED`).
- Browser events add the session as WebDriver cookies after opening `/login`, and a render on the sign-in page is `LOGIN_SHOWN`.
- README states the bearer token's scope. The two soak runbooks match the new flow.

**Evidence.** A principal-table test runs every executor kind against one fake target and fails on any mutating call with no entry. Two mutations were checked by hand: a missing entry, and a switchboard call sent as the Operator. Both were caught. Secrecy tests search every outcome, log, header and `resume` record, stdout, stderr and the guest's call log for the password and both tokens. On host scratch servers (temp `TANGLECLAW_HOME`, PortHub ports 4310 and 4313, Caddy off PATH): a cookie plus CSRF leased 201, with no CSRF 403, with no cookie 403 `LAUNCH_BINDING_REQUIRED`, and a logged-out cookie got 401 (A4); the file-based curl sequence answered 200/201/200/200, then 401 after logout; setup with no login, then `add-login`, then `set-password` took the gate open, account-required, armed, with provisioning off, and a signed-in lease got 201. The password appears in either server log 0 times. The Caddy-present cutover was found by reading the code and was never run. Owed to the next guest dry run (A344 D8, owner TC-Lane-A): the engine, fault and Safari cycles in a real guest, including A3 (Add Cookie with `httpOnly`), and steps 5 and 6 end to end.

## 2026-10-19 — #2088: remove the mechanisms behind three intermittent full-suite failures

<!-- prawduct: type=fix | scope=w21b1-2088-intermittent-hardening -->

v5.34.0 Wave 21 chunk W21-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w21b1-2088-intermittent-hardening` from `origin/main` `7641eb92`. Closes #2088 (item 3 was closed by #1993). Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w21b1-2088-intermittent-hardening.md` (rev1 sha256 `bd60e07a…a3c0`, Architect A343, INT); charter v2.54 five-file lock.

**What changed.** `verifyRecord` (`lib/release-certification/host-publish.js`) passes `-c gc.auto=0 -c maintenance.auto=false` on every git call against its scratch repository, because a `fetch` there spawns `git maintenance run --auto`, which can still be writing while the removal walks the directory (`ENOTEMPTY`). The removal retries (`maxRetries: 5`, `retryDelay: 50`) and still throws if it keeps failing; it goes through a new `opts.rm` seam. `relay()` and its persistent `_relay/<key>.git` are unchanged (A343 D2). The four `sendKeys - behavioral` tests that slept 0.4 s and then looked for text the typed command line already holds now wait for that text as the output line just above a fresh prompt. The four `api-medusa` test servers that listened on every address bind `127.0.0.1`.

**Evidence.** Not reproduced as the original failing tests; each mechanism was shown and removed. E1: with a server on `listen(0)`, a second process bound `127.0.0.1` on its port and answered its requests; on `listen(0, '127.0.0.1')` that bind fails `EADDRINUSE`. E2, through `verifyRecord`'s own wrapper with `GIT_TRACE=1`: no maintenance spawn; with the flags stripped, one. Mutations: dropping the flags, the retries, or the removal on the unreadable-remote return each fails the new #2088 tests. A command typed but never run passes the old `includes` check and fails the new wait. Under 20 CPU hogs, the restored 0.4 s sleep did not fail in 5 runs, so the load race itself is reasoned, not shown. Each changed test file passed 5 of 5 runs alone. Cumulative Critic `rev-20261019T014803Z-b89aa925`: 0 blocking, 1 warning and 3 notes, all accepted. The first full suite failed one test, `auto-merge-callers`, because a new comment quoted git's maintenance command with its auto flag; the comment was reworded, and `verify-resolutions` `rev-20261019T021409Z-9ffce600` found nothing.

## 2026-10-18 — W20-B1: the launch-binding dispatcher test holds every port it leases

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w20b1-launch-binding-port-fixture -->

v5.34.0 Wave 20 chunk W20-B1, chunk 1 of 1, Charter v2.52, Architect A334. Closes #2504. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w20b1-launch-binding-port-fixture.md` (rev1 sha256 `fe8c5f59…feb38`, INT `26faad2b`).

**What changed.** `test/launch-binding-dispatch.test.js` leased fixed ports counted up from 39401. In CI run 39170225170, another worker's listener on 39409 made the lease route refuse the "superseded" case's current-launch request as `409 PORT_IN_USE` before the binding verdict was reached. Now each lease binds a loopback port the OS chooses and holds it until `after()`, which closes every reservation even when a test fails. The request says the listener is its own with `adoptListener: true`, which the route reads only after the binding floor admits the caller. A failed bind throws a named fixture error. A new self-test asserts that two leases get distinct ports and that each is still bound (`EADDRINUSE`). No existing assertion changed, and no product file changed. With a stranger on 39409, and with strangers on 39401-39414, the file passes. Dropping `adoptListener` turns all five granted-lease cases red. Restoring only the fixed counter fails the self-test alone, because `adoptListener` would adopt a stranger's listener too. That makes the self-test the guard against losing the hold. Restoring the old fixture in full fails the self-test and, with a stranger on that case's port, the superseded case.

## 2026-10-18 — W19-B1: the twenty-wedged-reads case proves the loop turns while every read is in flight

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w19b1-twenty-wedged-reads-proof -->

v5.34.0 Wave 19 chunk W19-B1, chunk 1 of 1, Charter v2.51, Architect A326. Closes #2496. Refs #2200. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w19b1-twenty-wedged-reads-proof.md` (rev1 sha256 `cd7a65e1…2d19`, INT `2b730b48`).

**What changed.** `test/tmux-read-pane-async.test.js`'s "twenty wedged reads leave the event loop turning while they are waited on" case counted 10 ms timer turns while twenty reads ran out a 600 ms timeout, so slow timer delivery alone could fail it. Now the read timeout is 60 s, the stand-in's `hang` sleeps 120 s, and the test releases the reads by killing the children `countSpawns` recorded. It asserts that all 20 children exist and are alive, with no read settled, when the calls return. After at least 10 turns under a 60 s failure guard it asserts the same again, then that every released read rejects as `tcTimedOut`, each stopped at its probe, and every child is gone. The settled flags ride one `Promise.allSettled` that is subscribed at once, so a read that ends early fails a named assertion and never becomes an unhandled rejection. Load can now only make it slower. A blocking-read mutant fails "a read ended before the test released it"; a starved-interval mutant fails on 0 turns. `lib/tmux.js` is unchanged (no product defect). Node's own `execFile` timeout stays proven by the file's other `hang` cases, which use 300 ms timeouts.

## 2026-10-18 — #1903 Chunk 08, #2412: ten more lift copies use the shared helper, and the early-timer deadline case no longer depends on host speed

<!-- prawduct: type=refactor | scope=w18-a1-lift-chunk08-fingerprint-timer -->

v5.34.0 Wave 18 chunk W18-A1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/1903-2412-w18-a1` from `origin/main` `494a4897`. Refs #1903 (Chunk 08); closes #2412. **#2412.** `test/checkout-fingerprint.test.js` "reports a timeout as the deadline even when the timer fires before the clock reaches it" sent every unstalled call to real git under a 300 ms deadline. With git slowed by 0.1 s per spawn, five of the six stalls were never reached, and the deadline ended a real git process instead. The case now records real git's answers once, before the shifted-timer window. Inside the window it replays them (an unrecorded call throws) and freezes `Date.now`. It also asserts that each stalled call was ended exactly once. Logging at the seam and at the binary shows no git spawned inside the window. The case passed 200 runs unloaded and 50 beside ten CPU-bound processes. The 2026-10-14 failure itself was never reproduced (its assertion text was lost), so this removes the host-speed path rather than proving that path was the cause.

**#1903 Chunk 08.** `session-update-poll`, `session-wrap-finalize`, `session-wrapper` (five inline loops and a nested `functionBody`), `sessions` and `setting-disposition` drop their private brace-matching copies for `test/helpers/lift.js`. ## 2026-10-18 — W18-B1: the thirty-wedged-reads case proves the loop turns while every read is in flight

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w18b1-wedged-reads-proof-and-bridge-times -->

v5.34.0 Wave 18 chunk W18-B1, Charter v2.50, Architect A316. Closes #2200. Refs #2313.

**What changed.** `test/medusa-wake-async.test.js`'s "thirty wedged reads answering together leave the event loop turning, with real child processes" case counted 10 ms timer turns inside a ~500 ms read timeout, so host load alone could fail it: 2 turns, measured under background QoS with 120 CPU hogs, while every read was still judged. Now each stand-in child records its PID and sleeps past a 60 s guard, the read timeout is 60 s, and the test releases the reads by killing the children. It asserts that no reader has a verdict when the tick returns; that the loop turned at least 10 times with all 31 children started, still alive and unjudged; and, after release, that all 31 (30 sessions and the Master) are judged `pane-capture-failed`, nothing was typed, no blocking tmux call ran on the answer path and every child is gone. Load can now only make it slower. `lib/` is unchanged (no product defect). Node's own `execFile` timeout firing is no longer exercised in this case and stays proven by `test/tmux-read-pane-async.test.js` ("rejects as timed out…", "the timeout bounds the whole read"). The sibling case in that file has the same wall-clock shape and is filed as #2496.

**#2313** shipped in PR #2432 (`fb7f79fa`). No code here. Its signed-in after-deploy panel check stays owed (owner TC-Lane-B).

**Verified.** Case alone 5/5; 5/5 under 30 foreground CPU hogs; file all green; a scratch synchronous `readPaneAsync` (never committed) fails with "a wedged child ended before the test released it"; `sync-claude-md-block.js` compare exit 0.

## 2026-10-18 — #2489, #2184, #2046: a member's shared-doc lock is taken in its verified name with a bounded ttl, the bridge-gateway tests stop racing their clock and sockets, and the finalize docs say what shipped

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w17b1-lock-caller-binding-gateway-determinism-finalize-docs -->

v5.34.0 Wave 17 chunk W17-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w17b1-lock-caller-binding-gateway-determinism-finalize-docs` from `origin/main` `6ae92519`. Closes #2489, #2184, #2046. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w17b1-lock-caller-binding-gateway-determinism-finalize-docs.md` (rev2 sha256 `4796821b…61a1`, Architect A302/A304, INT `006884ff`); Charter v2.47 (sha256 `db13f4aaa53cc418318d39a2fc8d3c04301728b4e412127e77430bb0f95f6d04`), 15-file lock, 13 changed.

**#2489.** `POST /api/shared-docs/:id/lock` used to record whatever `sessionId` and `projectName` its body named. It now treats them as a member's claim and checks it against the launch binding: a mismatch is `403 LOCK_CALLER_MISMATCH` and nothing is written; a match records the verified session and project. The operator still names any session. `ttlMinutes` must be a whole number from 1 to 120 for every HTTP caller (`400 BAD_REQUEST`), and defaults to 30 when omitted. `documentLocks.acquire` is unchanged and still takes any ttl, which a new store test pins. The attest-route test that planted a foreign lock over HTTP now seeds it in the store; its assertions are unchanged. The `attestLockRefusal` JSDoc and the DELETE route comment no longer say every lock records the body's session.

**#2184.** The expiry test's "one millisecond inside" check anchored its clock to the newest question in the database, which a question an earlier test asked under a moved clock can become on a slow host. That would push the anchor past this question's real deadline. The test now anchors its three clock moves to its own route's question (`questionLater`). The fetch helpers in `test/api-bridge-gateway.test.js` and `test/api-bridge.test.js` send `connection: close`, so a pooled keep-alive socket the server is closing cannot be reused and read as ECONNRESET. Two named mechanisms are removed; the cause of every recorded failure is not proven. No assertion changed.

**#2046.** `docs/session-finalize.md` now says finalize makes no git *write*: its strict check fetches and prunes. The v5.30 status row for #2027 gives its exact head, `2074e241` (PR #2043). FEATURES.md and the CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` correction ride W17-REC.

**Guides.** Under Charter v2.46 and v2.47, `data/shared-docs-guide.md` and `docs/user-guide.md` now say a lock's `sessionId` and `projectName` must be the caller's own verified values and give the `ttlMinutes` range, and the tracked `CLAUDE.md` block is regenerated from the guide by `node scripts/sync-claude-md-block.js --write` (inside the markers only), so the guide note that round 1's R-3 routed to PM triage was done in this chunk instead.

**Critic.** Cumulative review, 0 blocking.

**rev-20261018T130705Z-79e55f32** — 2026-10-18T13:08:24Z

| Finding | Severity | State | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | warning | accepted | Stale suite evidence: the full suite runs once on the final head in the Integrator's suite slot (rule 264.4) and is recorded then; no slot was granted at review time. (Re-recorded: an earlier record put the change-log reason on this finding by an array-index slip.) |
| R-2 | warning | accepted | Missing change-log entry: the Integrator serializes .prawduct/change-log.md prepends; the slot was granted 13:12Z and the entry is prepended in this chunk's final head. (Re-recorded: an earlier record put the guide-text reason on this finding by an array-index slip.) |
| R-3 | note | accepted | Both files sit outside the chartered 12-file lock (the CLAUDE.md block is generated from data/shared-docs-guide.md; docs/user-guide.md is unlocked). Routed to PM triage as plan open item R-1, widened to name docs/user-guide.md and the tc whoami Session id pointer. |

**3 findings** (2 warning, 1 note) — accepted: 3.

**#1351.** `lib/dir-scanner.js` owns `fullDiskAccessHint(err, instead)` beside `failureCode`: only `tcTimedOut` earns the sentence, and the caller supplies only its action phrase ("choose a projects directory" in `lib/projects.js`, "move the project" in `lib/project-facts.js` and `lib/uploads.js`). `uploads.js#_hintFor` is gone; `_scanFailureHint` stays as a one-line delegate that keeps returning `undefined` on a miss. The setup wizard's fixture is built from `_scanFailureHint` instead of a hand-kept copy, same bytes.

**#1903 Chunk 06.** Ten test files drop their private brace matcher for `liftDeclaration` or `liftBody` from `test/helpers/lift.js`, and their ten `INITIAL_INVENTORY` entries go with them (56 → 46). A runtime capture logged the same lifted text for every call before and after, and the same closing-brace index in `openclaw-cache`, which now also asserts that index lands on the brace (an off-by-one either way was green before). Apart from that one added assertion, no assertion, test name or test count changed in the ten files; four copies that previously searched from index 0, sliced to `-1` or returned `''` on a miss now fail with the helper's message, which only an already-broken test sees.

## 2026-10-18 — #2290, #2484, #2485: only the holder or the operator releases a shared-doc lock, both launch 201s carry the same keys, and a Bridge-absent toggle test that no network can flip

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w16b1-doc-lock-holder-webui-201-bridge-seam -->

v5.34.0 Wave 16 chunk W16-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w16b1-doc-lock-holder-webui-201-bridge-seam` from `origin/main` `0d33fab9`. Closes #2290, #2484, #2485. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w16b1-doc-lock-holder-webui-201-bridge-seam.md` (rev1 sha256 `b5583901…c739`, INT `ae55b3a1`, Architect A290); charter v2.43 (sha256 `21ebf9cc…340e`), 10-file lock, of which `docs/engine-guide.md` and `test/master-launch-mode.test.js` are unchanged.

**#2290.** `DELETE /api/shared-docs/:id/lock` let any member session in the group drop any lock. A member session now releases only a lock it holds, judged by the session its launch binding verified, because the lock route records a body-supplied session id. New `documentLocks.releaseHeld` does the holder-or-expired check and the delete in one statement. Another member gets `409 NOT_LOCK_HOLDER`, the code the attest route already uses, with `holder: {sessionId, project, lockedAt, expiresAt}`, and the lock stays. The operator still releases any lock; an expired or absent lock answers `200`; a session's locks are still all released when it ends. The body-trusted `sessionId` on the lock route is #2489.

**#2484.** The web UI launch `201` now carries `launchMode`, the mode its session row records (the requested one only when the ClawBridge pre-create succeeded), and `isolationOverride: null`, because no engine starts on this host. A key-parity case gives the web UI `201` exactly the tmux `201`'s keys, so a key added to one branch alone fails. `docs/configuration-reference.md` documents both keys and the release rule.

**#2485.** The Bridge-absent toggle test pointed the probe at a port pair it had released, which a parallel run could reuse, and a probe deadline missed under load reads `BRIDGE_UNKNOWN`. The probe now runs the real classifier through transports that refuse at once; a second case with transports that answer healthy gets `BRIDGE_OK`, so the verdict comes from the supplied probe. The five original assertions are unchanged. The port pair now only gives the started listener somewhere other than a real Bridge to dial. The recorded failure's own cause stays unknown; its junit was not kept.

**Evidence.** Each new #2290 and #2484 case was red on base `server.js` and green after. Narrow runs: store, route and neighbouring shared-doc suites 104/104; launch `201` and neighbours 129/129; `api-medusa` 207/207; suites that read the configuration reference 136/136. The absent case passed 3 of 3 with ten CPU burners on ten cores. Critic `rev-20261018T101931Z-fff508ba`: 0 blocking. Full suite owed in INT's slot.
