## 2026-10-18 — #2059: a Codex 0.161/0.162 pane still drawing a turn is held under an idle thread, and the settling hold restarts when a turn ends

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w14b1-2059-04-codex-post-turn-guard -->

v5.34.0 Wave 14 chunk W14-B1, #2059 Chunk 04, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w14b1-2059-04-codex-post-turn-guard` from `origin/main` `dd8d28f0`. Closes #2059 (PM + Architect A269/A272 ruling D1(b), with the disclosure below). Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w14b1-2059-04-codex-post-turn-guard.md` (rev1 sha256 `dbadb07c…640a`, frozen); charter v2.39 (sha256 `8b4ebe21…edb9c`), whose 13-file lock adds three files the plan missed: `test/engine-wake-profiles.test.js` and `test/medusa-wake-engine-activity.test.js` pinned the old behaviour, and `docs/configuration-reference.md` described it.

**What changed.** `data/engines/codex.json` gives 0.161.0 and 0.162.0 each its own `restRows.<version>.turnEnd`: a turn row (`^[›•] `), end rows (`^  Worked for \d`, `^■ Conversation interrupted`) and its own evidence. `lib/medusa-wake.js` validates and compiles the member, and `_paneTurnInFlight` makes `_assessPane` answer `turn-in-flight` (`pane-turn-in-flight`) when a turn row is above the last bare composer and the last row above it is not an end row, for the exact version the channel proved. An older empty composer row is not a turn row: found when `test/medusa-wake-engine-activity.test.js`'s stale-opening case went red. The settling hold now also restarts when an idle answer follows a `busy` one on the same channel; an `unknown` in between does not. On 0.156.1 that delays the first nudge after an observed turn by one interval and never types sooner.

**Tests.** The two fixture files carry the whole signed-in watch timeline (every distinct frame once, per run the frame order, the launch thread's status and whether the client was stopped; same-length placeholder for the scratch path; sources by sha256), distilled by a script kept in the gitignored evidence folder. The replay runs every recorded frame through the real scan: nothing is typed into a stopped frame or one still drawing a turn, and the stopped run is next nudged under the drawn end of the turn; with the guard's call removed it types into a stopped frame. The streaming panes leave the known-unsafe list. The pins that described the old behaviour are rewritten to the new one, and nothing is weakened. Stopped records number 48, not the planned 42: 0.161.0's A run writes `SIGCONT` on the `ps` line, and the planning count missed that window. The hazard count (thread idle under a stopped frame) is 28, as planned.

**Limits.** No session of 0.161.0 or 0.162.0 is woken: `startupControl.verifiedVersions` stays `["0.156.1"]`, so the guard is dormant until one is admitted. Admission is tracked under OPEN #2222. The guard reads only the visible pane: an answer long enough to scroll its one opening `• ` row off the screen, then left still, is not refused. The recorded stops came about a dozen rows in, so that case was not measured; #2222's criterion is that a stall deeper than one screen is measured and refused before either version is listed. The re-arm needs mail waiting: a turn seen by nobody does not restart the hold.

## 2026-10-18 — #2469, #2253: the last review id leaves `lib/master.js`, and recovery test files exit when their last test ends

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w12b1-review-id-and-recovery-exit -->

v5.34.0 Wave 12 chunk W12-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w12b1-review-id-and-recovery-exit` from `origin/main` `bdc1a8bb`, with `origin/main` `bd1c1555` (W12-A1, PR #2474) merged in. Closes #2469 and #2253. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w12b1-review-id-and-recovery-exit.md` (rev2 sha256 `f32d6af1…77e0`, INT and Architect A250); charter v2.36 (sha256 `c64b776f…c0`).

**#2469.** `lib/master.js`'s guard-posture comment loses ` (R-15)`, the only `(R-n)` left in the file; the sentence is otherwise unchanged. `server.js:4690` still carries `(R-13)`; it is outside this chunk's files and reported to INT (D5).

**#2253: cause.** `launchStubbed` stubs tmux and engine detection only for the launch call, but the launch defers a boot watch, a kickoff and a bootstrap past it. The kickoff then polls a pane that never existed every 750 ms until `IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS` (90 s), on a referenced timer, so each file using the fixture waited ~90 s after its last test.

**#2253: fix.** `lib/launch-kickoff.js`'s idle-poll sleep is unref'd: a pending kickoff never holds a process open by itself, and in the server the listener keeps the loop alive, so production behaviour is unchanged. `test/_recovery-fixture.js` holds no-pane stand-ins while a temp store is open: the kickoff answers `not-silent`, the bootstrap `fired`, and the boot watch reads the pane as gone; `restore()` puts all four back.

**Walls** (each file alone, `node --test`, no forced exit, all 8 run in parallel on this host; before measured on the unfixed branch): clear-batch 141 → 40 s, held 112 → 19 s, batch-read 100 → 9 s, clearance-audit 99 → 8 s, project-recovery-mode-control 95 → 4 s, fleet-recovery-panel 93 → 2 s, clear-one 4 → 3 s, clear-ended 2 → 2 s. After the fix, each file's wall equals its own `duration_ms` to within a second. With the kickoff unref alone (the fixture reverted) every file also exits when its last test ends, but its tests run longer (held 28.7 s, clear-batch 54.0 s), because the real kickoff and bootstrap still run.

**Tests.** `test/launch-recovery-held.test.js` gains three cases: the 20 s exit bound stays below `IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`; a child `node` that launches through the fixture, waits until the deferred kickoff and bootstrap have both started, then restores, exits by itself within the bound (measured 0.4 s); and a child that only awaits `_internal.sleep(IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS)` exits within the bound (0.06 s). Red first on the unfixed tree: both child cases were killed at 20 s. With the fixture fixed and the unref reverted, the sleep pin fails and the launch pin passes. With the unref kept and the fixture reverted, both pass. So either change alone lets the launch child exit, the sleep pin alone tells them apart, and the fixture's stand-ins are measured by the walls above, not by a pin.

**Plan premise corrected (D2).** The plan expected a host with no tmux to leave the boot watch polling its 45 s window, which would have made the fixture's boot-watch stand-in load-bearing for the exit pin. Measured with `tmux` removed from the child's `PATH`: the unfixed fixture's boot watch still ended at `pane-gone` in 6 ms, because `tmux.probeSession` treats any non-timeout failure, including the shell's "command not found", as an answer that the session is gone. The stand-in is kept as approved, because the fixture's premise is that no pane exists.

**Narrow runs** on `15b2e89c`, all passing: the 8 fixture users, `launch-kickoff`, `launch-bootstrap-wiring`, `startup-dialog`, `startup-control-launch`, `master`, `master-control-bar`, `master-guard-source`.

## 2026-10-18 — #2470, #1903 Chunk 02: the changelog gate judges the commit a dormant methodology allows, and five lift copies use the shared helper

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w12-a1-coverage-withheld-prefixes-and-lift-migration -->

v5.34.0 Wave 12 chunk W12-A1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/2470-1903-w12-a1` from `origin/main` `bdc1a8bb`. Closes #2470; refs #1903 (Chunk 02). **#2470.** On an engine that cannot run Prawduct, the commit step and `session-files` withhold `.prawduct/`, but `_sortForCommit` classified with no withheld prefixes, so a withheld edit to a changelog declared under `.prawduct/` could satisfy coverage for source work the commit ships. `evaluate` now takes `options.methodology`, and `_sortForCommit` classifies with `governance.withheldPrefixesFor(extra.methodology || null)`, the commit step's own derivation. `ai-content.js` passes `context.methodology || null` from both `_verifyChangedGate` call sites through `_satisfactionPredicateGate` and `_changelogCoveragePredicate` (one trailing optional parameter each, D2). It is read from the run's context and never from `options`, which a request body supplies. With no methodology nothing is withheld, as before.

**#1903 Chunk 02.** `_page-globals.js` (`liftDeclaration`, still exported as the shared one, D5), `account-self-service-ui`, `awareness-observability`, `behind-origin` and `bind-notice-render` lift through `test/helpers/lift.js`; `INITIAL_INVENTORY` loses exactly those five entries. `account-self-service-ui` keeps the slice's line head and `behind-origin` its optional `async ` through short adapters with no brace counting. A scratch script hashed all 25 lifted strings on `bdc1a8bb` and on the new tree: identical (D6). Failure messages are now the helper's (D7).

**Plan delta.** The plan's parity case put the project below the repo root and the withheld path at the root's `.prawduct/`, which the project-relative offset drops whatever is withheld, so it could not go red. The case now checks parity for a project at the repo root (red before the fix) and below it.

**Tests.** `test/wrap-changelog-coverage.test.js`: a withheld changelog edit leaves `app.js` uncovered; a runnable or absent methodology stays covered; `excluded` equals the commit step's `notCommitted` at both depths. `test/wrap-step-ai-content.test.js`: on both run paths the predicate receives `context.methodology` itself, and `null` when only `options` carries one. Red first on `main`; mutations (no `withheldPrefixes`, a hard-coded dormant capability, reading `context.options`, one call site only, an adapter without the line head) each went red.

## 2026-10-17 — #2160, #2465: the wrap's file classifier derives the not-committed and scan lists itself, and the changelog gate uses the one wrap-subject rule

<!-- prawduct: type=refactor | scope=w11-a1-wrap-subject-import-and-derived-path-lists -->

v5.34.0 Wave 11 chunk W11-A1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/2465-2160-w11-a1` from `origin/main` `1a70ccd5`. Closes #2160 (items 1 and 3; item 2 shipped in PR #2161) and #2465. **#2160.** Two consumers rebuilt lists from the classifier's buckets by hand, so each new bucket had to be added to both, and a miss failed silently (#2154 missed the secret check). `classify` now returns `notCommitted`, every dirty path not in `stageable` in status order, and `committableOrAsked`, built by the exported `committableOrAskedOf`: `stageable`, then the foreign paths, then the session files left, without repeats. It also carries `paths`, so `reclassify` keeps both lists consistent. `_secret-check.js#check` scans `committableOrAsked` with no fallback. `changelog-coverage.js#_sortForCommit`'s `excluded` is `notCommitted` made project-relative, and `_excludedFromCommit`'s JSDoc says so relationally. Relational `notCommitted` differs from coverage's old five-bucket list only by `methodologyWithheld`, which is always empty there (O1 below), so no gate output changes.

**#2465.** `changelog-coverage.js` drops its own `WRAP_SUBJECT_RE` and `_isWrapCommit` body; the `_isWrapCommit` export is `gitRange.isWrapSubject` itself. The W10-A1 parity case becomes an identity case.

Option A (INT, Architect A240): the helper derives `committableOrAsked` through `committableOrAskedOf`; no assertion changed. D6 (PM charter v2.34, Architect A240, INT bound a ninth file): `_git-range.js`'s JSDoc no longer says `changelog-coverage` keeps its own copy; a comment-only change.

## 2026-10-17 — #2164, #2036, #1640, #1998: the savepoint helper is named for what it does, the absent-Bridge test no longer borrows an ephemeral port, review ids leave test names, and the create routing test stops racing a real child

<!-- prawduct: type=chore | scope=w11b1-savepoint-rename-port-and-test-hygiene -->

v5.34.0 Wave 11 chunk W11-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w11b1-savepoint-rename-port-and-test-hygiene` from `origin/main` `1a70ccd5`. Closes #2164, #2036, #1640, #1998. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w11b1-savepoint-rename-port-and-test-hygiene.md` (rev3 sha256 `a9e16605…50fe`, approved by INT and Architect A239-r3); charter v2.33 (sha256 `59b2d3e4…7d46`).

**#2164.** `lib/store.js` `_startupControlAtomic` is now `_withSavepoint`, and its savepoint `startup_control_atomic` is now `with_savepoint`, at the definition and all five call sites. Its JSDoc names both kinds of caller: the startup-control trims and the session-rule replacement paths. No behaviour change.

**#2036.** The "toggle {enabled:true} does NOT refuse when the Bridge is absent" test pointed the Bridge at 59991, in macOS's ephemeral range. The preflight also probes the derived WebSocket port, 59992, so a process holding either turned `BRIDGE_ABSENT` into another code. The test now binds an OS-chosen port and its +1 neighbour, releases both, and points the Bridge there.

**#1640.** Twelve review ids deleted: ten test names in `test/master.test.js`, `test/master-control-bar.test.js` and `test/master-guard-source.test.js`; one assertion message in `test/api-medusa.test.js`; and the same defect in `test/release-certification-host-publish.test.js`, which is #1640's failure family under charter §1.4(h). Per-file test counts are unchanged. #2469 stays open for INT to reconcile.

**#1998.** "runs on the scanner the background poll cannot kill underneath it" (`createProjectsDir`) stubbed only `dirScanner.request`, so the create ran a real child under the 5 s deadline. Both entry points are now stubbed and counted, as #1993 did for the wizard scan. The wizard-scan `withScanner` `@param` now names `interactiveRequest`. The audit the issue asks for: five other `createProjectsDir` tests run a real child, and they stay real, because the child's filesystem result is what they assert; the four refusal tests return before any child runs.

**Limits.** #2036: another process can still take the port pair between its release and the preflight. #1998: the five filesystem `createProjectsDir` tests can still overrun 5 s on a heavily loaded host.

**Tests.** Red-first: with 59991 and 59992 held, the old Bridge test failed (`BRIDGE_UNHEALTHY`), and the new one passed. Pointing `createProjectsDir` at `dirScanner.request` turned the create routing test red, and so did removing its interactive stub. Narrow ring: the 48 test files that touch the tables the five savepoint callers write, plus the six edited test files. All green.

## 2026-10-17 — #1280, #1903: a session's files: stamp no longer carries an earlier wrap commit's paths, and one shared lift helper replaces the private brace-matchers going forward

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w10-a1-wrap-commit-subtraction-and-lift-helper -->

v5.34.0 Wave 10 chunk W10-A1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/1280-1903-w10-a1` from `origin/main` `0eb92b05`. Closes #1280; refs #1903 (Chunk 01). Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/w10-a1-wrap-commit-subtraction-and-lift-helper-1280-1903.md` (rev1 sha256 `8c9d6df9…7853`, approved by INT `bdd5fe33` and Architect A229); charter v2.32 (sha256 `57e66a84…d025`).

**#1280.** `continuity-write` records `files:` over the session range, which opens at the previous wrap commit's parent (#664), so it always held that wrap commit, and a wrap commits the whole tree. A session range with an earlier wrap commit in it is now walked commit by commit and every wrap commit is dropped except the one this wrap made (all of them when it made none). A path an earlier wrap commit and a real commit both changed stays. One delta still feeds the Map and the stamp. `_git-range.js` gains `WRAP_SUBJECT_RE`/`isWrapSubject` and `hasEarlierWrapCommitsAsync`; a parity test holds `changelog-coverage`'s copy of the rule to the same answers. Two choices the plan did not settle, both approved by Architect A231 at `00ba23e4`: only a session range subtracts, so the trunk fallback (published only when no wrap has ever stamped) keeps the branch's divergence as measured; and a refused walk logs the diff path's "session delta was refused" line, while a killed one keeps its own line. Follow-up #2465 moves `changelog-coverage` onto the shared rule.

**#1903 Chunk 01.** New `test/helpers/lift.js`: `liftDeclaration`, `liftBody`, `loadEsc`, `loadJsArg`. `test/lift-helper.test.js` pins the copies' exact behaviour, the string/comment brace limitation included, proves the loaders run the shipped `esc`/`jsArg` text from `landing.js` and `session.js`, and freezes the inventory of private copies (90 lifters, 3 named exceptions) as an exact multiset. No existing test file migrates here.

**Limits.** Wrap commits are matched by subject prefix: a hand commit starting `Session wrap` is subtracted too, as `changelog-coverage` already does. A refused probe keeps the plain diff, which over-reports by the earlier wrap commit as before; a killed one is named. `changelog-coverage.js` keeps its own copy of the rule until the follow-up issue lands. The inventory rule finds the `indexOf('{')` brace-matcher shape only.

**Tests.** `test/wrap-continuity-session-provenance.test.js` (real git): new subtraction, shared-path, walk-beside-a-trunk-sync cases; the four D5 assertions re-based. `test/wrap-step-continuity-write.test.js`: subject table, parity, probe refused/killed, walk subtraction, fallback untouched. Mutations: no subtraction, subtracting this wrap's own commit, diff-minus-paths, a re-implemented loader and a new private copy each went red.

## 2026-10-17 — #2008, #2155: the wrap's commit step skips a folder that is not a git repository, and records the Include / Leave answers it ran with

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w10b1-commit-non-git-skip-and-answers-audit -->

v5.34.0 Wave 10 chunk W10-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w10b1-commit-non-git-skip-and-answers-audit` from `origin/main` `0eb92b05`. Closes #2008 and #2155. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w10b1-commit-non-git-skip-and-answers-audit.md` (rev 1, sha256 `97becd6e…5456`, approved by INT and Architect A228); charter v2.32 (sha256 `57e66a84…d5fe025`).

**#2008.** On a project whose folder is not a git repository, `commit` blocked at `git status` (exit 128), so `continuity-write`, `apply-pr-resolutions` and `handoff-stage` never ran. The step now skips there, after flushing the staged writes (so the wrap's files are written in place) and before any git command, with a reason saying the project gets no commit or PR history. The scope tells the two null-toplevel cases apart: no `workTreeProblem` is no repository and skips; a `workTreeProblem` is git failing to answer and still blocks. That guard moved up from below `git status` with the skip, unchanged in text, so a git failure is never read as no repository. A missing scope reads as no repository; production always passes one.

**#2155.** Each commit and each skip writes one `wrap.commit_answers` activity row: `outcome`, the `reason` (skipped) or `commitSha` (committed), the paths answered Include and Leave as given, and `committedOwn`, the session's own paths that were committed. Path names only. A blocked commit writes none. A new event type on the existing table: no schema change.

**Limits.** A non-git project still gets no commit or PR history, by design. The answers are those the wrap's request carried; an answer the drawer never sent is absent. Path lists are uncapped (D4).

**Tests.** `test/wrap-pipeline.test.js`: T1 a real non-repository skips with its staged file on disk and no git call; T2 no scope does the same; T3 `workTreeProblem` still blocks after the flush; T5 a pipeline run on a non-repository reaches `continuity-write`, `apply-pr-resolutions` and `handoff-stage`. `test/wrap-step-commit-autopr.test.js`: T6 a commit's row (answers as given, committed own paths, no file content); T7 the clean-tree, all-Left and non-repository skips; T8 no row when blocked; T9 one row per run, with the session. Seven of the eight were red against `main`'s `commit.js`; T8 holds on both. The existing non-repo `git status` case (T4) is unchanged and passing.

## 2026-10-17 — #2206: a locked store gets one bounded retry and one diagnostic at the stored launch-blocker lookup, and a failed lookup is still never "no blocker"

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w9b1-stored-blocker-lookup-retry -->

v5.34.0 Wave 9 chunk W9-B1, one chunk. Branch `fix/w9b1-stored-blocker-lookup-retry` from `origin/main` `d06cd1e1`. Closes #2206. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w9b1-stored-blocker-lookup-retry.md` (rev 3, sha256 `b723c01e…fc9`, accepted by INT and Architect A220); charter v2.31 (sha256 `9918cf38…b532f`). Lock: `lib/tmux.js`, `lib/sessions.js`, `test/startup-dialog.test.js`, `test/sessions.test.js` (headroom, not touched), this file.

**The change.** Both send gates (`_startupDialogOn` in `lib/tmux.js`, and `_startupDialogAtSend`'s no-row path in `lib/sessions.js`) read the pane's stored launch blocker through one new exported `tmux.readStoredLaunchBlocker(tmuxName)`. A lock held by another connection (`SQLITE_BUSY`: `errcode` 5, or SQLite's busy message) is retried once after `STORED_BLOCKER_RETRY_WAIT_MS` (50 ms, a synchronous `Atomics.wait`, as the workload append's retry). No other failure is retried. A throw that remains is still carried as unknown, never as "no blocker", so a send goes through only on a positive prompt reading (A146). The per-send `warn` is replaced by a condition log keyed `stored-launch-blocker-unreadable:<class>`, with the class from a fixed list (`SQLITE_BUSY`, `SQLITE_IOERR`, `SQLITE_CORRUPT`, `SQLITE_FULL`, `SQLITE_ERROR`, `unknown`; extended codes map to their primary code; a thrown `code` is never a class): one `warn` per class per incident, `debug` while it persists, and a lookup that answers resolves every class. `_startupDialogOn` keeps its no-store-open guard; the launch path still has none, so a closed store is unknown there, not retried.

**Decisions (INT, as recommended).** D1 one retry after 50 ms; D2 no connection-wide `busy_timeout` (it would touch every store call); D3 the launch path's `store.sessions.get(sessionId)` read is not retried (a throw there already falls to the retried lookup).

**Residual.** A lock held past the retry still refuses sends to panes not at the prompt, by A146's rule; the diagnostic now names it once. While another connection holds the lock, each text send waits at most 50 ms more.

**Tests.** New cases only in `test/startup-dialog.test.js`; no existing assertion changed. T1 busy then answer (both callers), T2 busy twice (prompt sends; empty, undecided and unreadable panes, unnamed and non-declaring engines refuse; a dialog keeps its name), T3 a disk error and an uninitialised store are not retried, T4 a real `BEGIN EXCLUSIVE` on the test store's file (two lookups, refused, one `SQLITE_BUSY` warn; resolved after `ROLLBACK`), T5 one warn per class per incident and a fixed class list, T6 the wait is the constant and once per lookup, T7 the launch path's answers, T8 the launch path with no store open. The existing always-busy stubs now take one 50 ms wait longer each. The narrow run covered every test file that loads or reads `lib/tmux.js` or `lib/sessions.js` (108 files): green.

## 2026-10-17 — #2450 + #2451: a failed boot-watch clear no longer stops a launch, and a stored launch blocker nobody reads is still kept current

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w8b1-launch-blocker-clear-and-sweep -->

v5.34.0 Wave 8 chunk W8-B1, chunks 01 and 02 of 2 on one branch. Branch `fix/w8b1-launch-blocker-clear-and-sweep` from `origin/main` `dd055aed`. Closes #2450 and #2451. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w8b1-launch-blocker-clear-and-sweep.md` (rev 2, sha256 `048eee77…0a7d`, accepted by INT); charter v2.30 (sha256 `02fa43c3…0892`, Architect A212). Lock: `lib/sessions.js`, `server.js` (two wiring lines), `test/sessions.test.js`, `test/startup-dialog.test.js`, this file.

**#2450.** In `_deferEngineInit`, the boot watch's `answered` branch called `clearLaunchBlocker` with no catch of its own. A store error there fell into the chain's outer catch, which logs "Launch boot watch failed" and withholds every launch send. The clear now has its own catch and logs a `warn` (`write: 'clear'`), and the launch goes on. Each send already looks at the pane before typing (`_startupDialogAtSend` → `_reconcileLaunchBlocker`): at the prompt that look clears the blocker and sends, and with the dialog still up it withholds. Any other throw after the watch still reaches the outer catch and withholds.

**#2451.** A stored blocker was corrected only by a reader (status read, project card, send). A session nobody read kept it after the dialog was answered, and `markCrashed` then recorded the dialog as the crash's `cause`. The crash cannot be checked when it is recorded, because by then the pane is gone. A new `reconcileStoredLaunchBlockers()` reads each ACTIVE session that has a stored blocker through `liveLaunchBlocker` while its pane is live, so it clears and keeps blockers by every reader's rules. A pane tmux says is dead, or did not answer for, is not read or written, and a session with no blocker costs no tmux call. It counts a blocker as cleared only when the stored row no longer holds it, re-read after the pane read: a clear that throws still answers null from the reader, so a failed clear is counted kept and retried on the next tick (INT's pre-suite finding on `e26acbc4`). One project's error is logged and the rest are still swept. `startLaunchBlockerSweep`/`stopLaunchBlockerSweep` run it every 15 s on an `unref`'d timer. `server.js` starts and stops it beside `launchUnready` (two lines), so a blocker persisted across a restart is swept by the first tick. The crash-time re-check, the other shape the issue names, was rejected: the pane is gone at every `markCrashed` call site.

**Residual.** A session whose dialog is answered and which crashes within one interval (≤15 s), before any reader, is still recorded with the stale cause. A pane that cannot be read keeps its blocker, by design. This closes the no-poll residual that W7-B1 (#2455) left open on #2451, down to that interval.

**Tests.** New cases only; no existing assertion changed. `test/startup-dialog.test.js` (#2450, the launch harness): a clear that throws once, so the launch types its pre-key, prime and kickoff, and the send-time look clears the record; a clear that always throws, where the launch types and the record stands; the dialog still up at send time, where nothing is typed and a launch send is refused; a throw elsewhere after the watch, which still withholds and records the skipped paste. Run against the old code, the first three fail and the fourth passes, as it should. `test/sessions.test.js` (#2451): the issue's case; a dialog kept; unread kept with no write; dead and unanswered panes not read; no blocker means no probe; one project failing while the next is swept; a clear that throws once is not counted cleared, the blocker stands, and the next sweep clears it with no stale crash cause; the timer lifecycle (one `unref`'d 15 s timer, idempotent start and stop); a tick runs the sweep; a blocker persisted across a restart cleared by the first tick, or kept with the dialog up. The narrow run covered every test file that loads `lib/sessions.js` or reads it or `server.js` as source (132 files): green.

**Not in this PR.** The owed tracked learnings file is outside W8-B1 and W8-REC (INT ruling); the PM schedules it.

**Review.** Cumulative Critic `rev-20261017T152544Z-e66f981e`: 0 blocking. After INT's pre-suite finding, verify-resolutions `rev-20261017T153422Z-37b5e94a` on the counting fix: 0 blocking, 3 observations (O-1 fixed by this paragraph's update, O-2 and O-3 accepted).

**rev-20261017T152544Z-e66f981e** — 2026-10-17T15:27:04Z

| Finding | Severity | State | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | warning | accepted | The full suite runs once on the frozen head in INT's suite slot (charter 1.5, plan freeze step 3); its run is recorded as test evidence before the ready report. The narrow run on this head (132 source-reading files, 5734 pass, 0 fail) is what the build stage allows. |
| R-2 | note | accepted | Owed, not dropped: the change-log entry is prepended at freeze step 2 and carries this review's id, so it follows the review. |
| R-3 | note | accepted | As the plan chose: a synchronous read matches launch-unready's monitor; a session with no stored blocker costs no tmux call, so a healthy fleet pays nothing. The tmux timeout bounds the worst case. |

**3 findings** (1 warning, 2 note) — accepted: 3.

## 2026-10-17 — #2452, #2453: the switchboard's own nudge is refused with a dialog's selector below its composer, and the docs describe the own-nudge rules

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w8-a1-own-nudge-below-composer -->

v5.34.0 Wave 8 chunk W8-A1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/2452-2453-w8-a1` from `origin/main` `dd055aed`. Closes #2452 and #2453. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/w8-a1-own-nudge-below-composer-2452-2453.md` (rev 1, sha256 `413c1db9…7740d`, approved by INT and Architect A211); charter v2.30 (sha256 `02fa43c3…0892`).

**#2452.** A Claude Code composer holding exactly the switchboard's own stranded nudge, with a dialog's selector (`❯ 1. Yes`) drawn below it and no older bare `❯` row in the read, was judged `at-prompt`: `_glyphRowBelowPrompt` anchors on the last bare row and there was none, and Claude declares no at-rest marker, so W7-A1's below-composer guard (inside the missing-marker excuse) never ran. The next wake would have cleared the composer and typed into a dialog. `_glyphRowBelowComposer(rows, profile, region)` now holds that check, and the excuse calls it with its verdict unchanged (`not-at-rest` stays pinned, ruling D1). The refusal branch of `_glyphRowBelowPrompt` also asks it whenever the own nudge is excused, with the same reason mapping (`composer-has-input` when the cursor shows input, else `no-prompt`; ruling D2). The same hole for Antigravity at rest, with its marker drawn, closes with it (ruling D3). Tests came first: the issue's repro, every profile drawing Claude's glyph without a marker, and Antigravity with its marker present were red (`at-prompt`) before the fix. Two positive pins (footer only below; a glyph-led transcript row above) stay `at-prompt`. Three mutations (no refusal; scan from the composer's first row; scan the whole read) each turn the expected tests red.

**#2453.** `docs/engine-guide.md` said the own nudge was excused while nothing glyph-led was below the last bare prompt; it now says below the composer, with or without a bare row. It and `docs/medusa-delivery.md` now describe W7-A1's missing-marker excuse for that nudge, and the engine guide also describes the boxed-composer anchor (#2358).

**Limits.** The Claude frame is constructed, not captured live. The fix only adds a refusal, so the risk is a held nudge, never a mistyped one. Codex's unbordered composer still never qualifies. The plan named three Claude profiles. The test store derives only `claude`, so the test loops over every profile with Claude's glyph and no marker, and asserts there is at least one.

## 2026-10-17 — #2204, #2194, #2196: a stored launch blocker is written safely, read without sleeping, and kept current on the card

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w7b1-launch-blocker-status-reconcile -->

v5.34.0 Wave 7 chunk W7-B1, chunk 1 of 1. Branch `fix/w7b1-launch-blocker-status-reconcile` from `origin/main` `474a6824`. Closes #2204, #2194 and #2196. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w7b1-launch-blocker-status-reconcile.md` (rev 2, sha256 `944e87f9…ffac`, accepted by INT); charter v2.27 (sha256 `4209d42e…6f29`, Architect A196). #2195 stays parked (`2195WAIT`). The boot watch's own writes (#2450) and the stale record when nothing reads it (#2451) are follow-ups, not in this chunk.

**#2204.** `_reconcileLaunchBlocker` called `setLaunchBlocker` and `clearLaunchBlocker` with no catch. It runs inside a launch's pre-key and prime timers, so a store write that threw escaped the timer: the send was neither made nor recorded as withheld. Each write now has its own catch and logs a `warn` naming the session, the write (`set`/`clear`) and the error. The pane read decides the answer: a failed set leaves the stored value, a failed clear still answers `null` because the pane showed the prompt, and the row stays as it was until the next reader writes it. The prime timer's existing withhold path then writes its `prime-paste` `skipped` row with the dialog's reason.

**#2194.** `check` re-read a half-drawn dialog frame after `execSync('sleep 0.25')`, which blocked the event loop on every status poll of such a frame, though a status read types nothing. `check`, `checkEngine` and `look` take `settle` (default on, so every sender keeps its re-read). A status read passes `settle: false`, is answered from the first frame, and a half-drawn frame is still `suspect`, which is never stored.

**#2196.** The project card served `row.launchBlocker` as stored, so a dialog answered in the pane kept the card saying the launch was waiting. A new `sessions.liveLaunchBlocker(active, project)` is the one statement of a reader's view: no pane read when no blocker is stored, else the reconcile with `settle: false`. `getSessionStatus` and `enrichProject` both use it, the card only on a pane tmux confirmed. `_liveSession` keeps its two-argument contract and serves the stored row; `enrichProject` then sets `session.launchBlocker` from `liveLaunchBlocker` when tmux confirmed the pane. This departs from the plan's third-argument design (Architect A202), so that the existing source guard on the literal `_liveSession(activeSession, wrapping)` call in `test/wrap-indicator-card.test.js` still holds. Cost: one synchronous pane capture (and a cursor read on some frames) per session with a stored blocker, per project-list poll, only after tmux confirms the pane; a session with no blocker costs nothing. The badge renderer is unchanged: it already hides on `null`.

**Tests.** New cases only; no existing assertion changed. The session-level cases are in `test/startup-dialog.test.js`, whose harness already holds the dialog frames and the launch timers (INT told before the suite), so `test/sessions.test.js` is unchanged. `check` with `settle: false` reads once and does not sleep; `look`/`checkEngine` pass the option through. A status read on a half-drawn frame does not sleep, while an injection and a launch send on the same frame each re-read once. A failed set or clear does not throw, logs, and leaves the answer to the pane, also through the status read. A dialog the store cannot record at the paste still withholds it and writes the `skipped` row. `liveLaunchBlocker` reads no pane with nothing stored, clears an answered dialog, and keeps one still up. `test/card-session-contract.test.js`: the card serves `null` for an answered dialog and clears the row; a pane tmux did not confirm is not read. `test/api-sessions.test.js`: the status route serves a stored blocker on a half-drawn frame without sleeping. Each new card and API case was run against the unchanged `lib/` and failed. Narrow runs on the final head: the changed test files, `test/wrap-indicator-card.test.js`, every other suite file that reads `lib/projects.js`, `lib/sessions.js` or `lib/startup-dialog.js` as source, and the files that read a launch blocker or the card (`projects`, `engine-error-surface`, `session-rule-delivery`, `stranded-wraps-ui`, `engine-error-ui`, `sessions`), each exiting 0.

**Docs.** `docs/engine-guide.md` no longer says the project list serves the stored record without reading the pane: it now checks a stored blocker against the pane whenever tmux confirmed it (lock widened to this file by charter v2.28, Architect A206).

**First head, held.** On the first head, `7b69b093`, exact-head CI failed on that guard (run 39034005054): the first version passed the checked blocker as a third argument to `_liveSession`, which broke the literal. The host full suite on that head recorded 12616 passed / 0 failed while `node --test` exited 1, and its output was not kept (#2456; a strong inference, not proven, is that it was the same test, with the recorder's JUnit parse missing a nested failure). The head was held (A201 Option A), `origin/main` `81532b76` was merged in, and the fix approved by A202 was applied.

**Review (first head).** Cumulative Critic `rev-20261017T122449Z-be7bf447`: 0 blocking; 2 findings (1 warning, 1 note), both accepted. The warning (no full-suite run for this head yet) is answered by the full suite on the frozen head in the Integrator's slot. The note (no backlog item resolved) is answered by the PR's `Closes` lines.

## 2026-10-17 — #2358 + #2447: a stranded nudge in an Antigravity composer is recognised; the sibling tmux tests wait for bash

<!-- prawduct: type=fix | scope=w7-a1-antigravity-wake-at-rest -->

v5.34.0 chunk W7-A1 (charter v2.27, Architect A198; plan rev 2 `dc9466b1` approved by INT and the Architect). Branch `fix/2358-2447-w7-a1` from `origin/main` `474a6824`.

**#2358 (a).** Antigravity hides its `? for shortcuts` hint while its composer holds text. A switchboard nudge that missed its Enter therefore removed the marker the wake gate required, and every later wake refused it as `not-at-rest` until it escalated. This was captured live at 167x27; one Enter submitted the nudge. `_assessPane` now excuses the missing marker only when all of these hold:

- the composer holds exactly the monitor's own nudge, seen whole between its borders;
- no glyph-led row is drawn below the composer;
- the hint slot at the left of the status row is empty (`_hintSlotEmpty`).

The last two conditions were not in the plan. The marker's absence is also what refused a dialog. agy draws no bare prompt row while its composer holds text, so `_glyphRowBelowPrompt` had nothing to anchor on, and a selector below the nudge read `at-prompt`. INT accepted the below-composer condition as a routed safety choice. The cumulative Critic then showed that the recorded dialog analog (`AG_DIALOG_PANE`), drawn above the composer, also got through. Both measured own-nudge captures leave the hint slot empty, and that dialog fills it with its key help, so any text in the slot now refuses. The busy marker and an agent fleet are never excused.

**#2358 (b).** A wrap that starts a continuation row with `>` made `locateComposer` take that row as the composer's first, so the gate and the prompt clear's residue check both missed the nudge. With a box in view, meaning a border above the cursor and one below it, the first row is now the glyph-led row directly under its upper border. The lower border is required so that a rule row in Codex's transcript cannot make the message under it the composer's head. With no box in view, the nearest glyph-led row decides, as before.

**#2447.** The sibling `sendKeys`/`sendRawKey` tests typed into bash before its prompt was drawn. On a loaded host they scored tty echo rather than what ran. Each test starts its own session and calls one `awaitBashPrompt` helper that waits for the prompt. The helper does not start the session itself, so the name passed to tmux stays traceable by the #1983 session-name guard. The clearPrompt-false, enter:false and raw-Enter tests assert on output lines and fail when mutated; four more tests gain only the wait. The `paste-buffer -p (#75)` test that the plan listed reads the source and starts no bash, so it is unchanged.

**Tests flipped, not weakened.** Two pins that recorded the known-bug verdicts now record the fixed ones, as the issue's Expected asks: the measured M1 capture's `(b) CHARACTERIZATION` (`not-at-rest` → `at-prompt`) and the `>`-wrap case (`no-prompt` → `at-prompt`). The M1 "diagnostic only" test now asserts that the channel's idle answer makes no difference.

## 2026-10-17 — #2152 + #831: the draft-text tmux test waits for bash, and the last test files that made repos on their own

<!-- prawduct: type=fix | scope=w6-a2-tmux-prompt-and-temp-repo -->

v5.34.0 chunk W6-A2 (charter v2.26, Architect A192; plan rev 3 approved by INT). Branch `fix/2152-831-w6-a2` from `origin/main` `98774fb0`. It carries W6-A1's five reviewed #831 commits as cherry-picks (W6-A1 stopped on the #2152 flake; PR #2443 closed as superseded) and adds the #2152 fix.

**#2152.** The test "does not submit unsent draft text sitting at the prompt (#812)" typed its draft 0.3 s after starting bash. On a loaded host bash had not drawn its prompt yet, so the tty echoed `echo DRAFTRAN` above the banner and `!content.includes('DRAFTRAN')` failed although nothing ran. It now waits, bounded at 10 s and failing with the last pane lines, for bash's prompt, then for the draft on the prompt line, then for the injected command's output and a fresh prompt. The pane-text check is replaced by line checks on what executed: some line is exactly `INJECTEDOK` and no line starts with `DRAFTRAN`. With `clearPrompt: false` (the #812 defect) the test goes red on `DRAFTRANecho INJECTEDOK`.

**#831.** 38 `git init`/`git clone` calls in 19 test files went through a local `git(cwd, ...args)`, `git(cwd, args)` or `run(args)` helper, so each one copied the machine's global `init.templateDir`, which a running TangleClaw rewrites under the suite. The family guard in `test/temp-repo.test.js` could not see those shapes.

**What changed.** The guard gains a pass keyed on a git wrapper's definition, matched over the file's text so a `return` on the line after the signature counts. It knows which parameter carries the argv, even when the argv array spreads several values (`['-C', dir, ...extra, ...args]`), so `git(dir, 'init')`, `git(dir, ['clone', …])` and `run(['init', …])` are caught and a non-git helper such as `mk(dir, 'clone')` or `npm(dir, 'init')` is not. It replaces the earlier rest-parameter-only definition pattern, so wrappers are recognised in one place. New positive and negative scanner tests cover it. With only the guard applied it failed on exactly those 38 calls. All 38 now call `initRepo`/`cloneRepo` with their own arguments, cwd and env. Five helpers built their env inline; it is hoisted into a `gitEnv()` the helper shares. No assertion changed. The `_temp-repo.js` header and the SHAPES comment now say what the guard catches and what it still cannot see.

**Not covered.** The guard still cannot see a subcommand held in a variable, a wrapper whose body does work before `return exec*('git', …)`, a wrapper imported from another module, an argv the wrapper spreads from an array it was given, a leading argument with nested calls, or a call split across lines. None of these is in `test/` today; the list lives beside the guard's SHAPES.

**Review.** Cumulative Critic `rev-20261017T104048Z-7d244602`: 0 blocking; 2 warnings and 3 notes, all accepted. The warnings (the #2152 race in the other `sendKeys - behavioral` tests) are outside the chartered scope and filed as #2447. The `_temp-repo.js` header note is left so the carried content stays identical to `be288eab`.

## 2026-10-17 — #2201: a damaged launch blocker is logged once instead of dropped silently

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w6b1-dialog-classifier-and-launch-blocker -->

v5.34.0 Wave 6 chunk W6-B1 02 of 2. Branch `fix/w6b1-02-launch-blocker-warn` from `6216a7e5`. Closes #2201. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w6b1-dialog-classifier-and-launch-blocker.md` (approved rev 2a, sha256 `f93d2dd8…2376`); charter v2.24 (sha256 `08269a39…c875f`, Architect A184). #2195 stays parked (`2195WAIT`): `setLaunchBlocker` and its docstring are unchanged.

**Why.** `_launchBlockerFromRow` returned `null` without a word for a `sessions.launch_blocker` value that did not parse or named no `code`, so a damaged row read as "nothing is blocking this launch" with no trace of why. It runs on every session read, so an unkeyed warning would repeat on every poll.

**What.** A present but unreadable value now logs one `warn` per (session id, raw value), naming the session id, the reason (`not JSON` / `no code`) and the value's length, never the value itself, and still returns `null`. The memory of what was warned about is a Set keyed by session id and a SHA-256 of the value, capped at `LAUNCH_BLOCKER_WARN_CAP` (1000) and cleared when full, so a long-running server cannot grow it without bound; `close()` clears it too, since another database opened in the same process reuses session ids. The cap is exported for its test as `_LAUNCH_BLOCKER_WARN_CAP`.

**Tests.** `test/store.test.js`, a new `describe` that captures the logger (`setConsoleStream`, `setLevel('warn')`, restored after): missing → `null` with no line; valid → parsed with no line; not JSON → one line naming session, reason and length but not the value, none on a re-read; parsed with no code → one line; a new damaged value on the same session → one more; the same value on another session warns for it; after `close()` and a reopen the same session id and value warn again; at the cap a remembered value stays quiet, and once cleared it warns again. The five new warning cases fail on the unchanged lib. No existing assertion changed. Narrow run: every test file that reads a launch blocker (six files), green, with no new log line seen by any of them.

## 2026-10-17 — #2202, #2203: the startup-dialog classifier reads a pane once; its non-breaking space is spelled as an escape

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w6b1-dialog-classifier-and-launch-blocker -->

v5.34.0 Wave 6 chunk W6-B1 01 of 2. Branch `fix/w6b1-01-dialog-classifier` from `896f6f72`. Closes #2202 and #2203. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w6b1-dialog-classifier-and-launch-blocker.md` (approved rev 2a, sha256 `f93d2dd8…2376`); charter v2.24 (sha256 `08269a39…c875f`, Architect A184). Chunk 02 (#2201) is a later session; #2195 is parked (`2195WAIT`).

**Why.** `detect`, `assessBoot` and `classify` in `lib/startup-dialog.js` decide whether a send's Enter could land in an engine's startup dialog. Each re-derived the same reading of a frame (plain text, prompt row, last marker row), `assessBoot` and `classify` did it twice per call by calling `detect` first, and a fourth scan found a partial marker's owner (#2202). Three copies of one reading can drift apart. Separately, `_plain`'s blank-collapsing class held a literal U+00A0, which looks like a space; an editor that normalised it would silently stop NBSP-padded marker words from matching (#2203).

**What.** One internal `_read(lines, dialogs, wake, cursor)` returns `{text, rows, prompt, lastMarker, dialog, owner}`; the three public functions are projections of it and keep their signatures, return shapes and the deliberate boot-vs-answer difference. Every dialog's last marker row comes from one pass up the rows (`_markerRows`, replacing `_lastMarkerRow`), so each check makes one marker scan however many dialogs are declared, keeping each dialog's own last-marker rule and the declaration order. `_read` treats a missing dialog list as empty, so `assessBoot`/`classify` no longer throw a TypeError on one (INT ruling; every caller passes `declared()`'s array); tests pin it for null and undefined. `_read` is not exported and there is no spy test: the helpers are called only inside `_read` (INT ruling). `_plain` spells the character as an escape, as `_norm` already did. The docstring half of #2202 was already fixed on main by `cdba432b`; the charter's `_assessEnginePane` is `assessBoot`.

**Evidence.** A behaviour pin (every named pane fixture in the test file, with and without its cursor and the prompt signature, against Claude's dialogs and a list adding a second dialog sharing a marker) was committed green on the unchanged lib (`6cd4b1fb`) and is unchanged and green after the refactor. A fixture pads the trust dialog's marker words with U+00A0; a source guard fails on a literal U+00A0 in `lib/startup-dialog.js` (checked by reintroducing it: 1 fail; restored). No existing assertion changed.

## 2026-10-17 — #2378: the landing page says so when no login is in force

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=w5b1-no-login-landing-notice -->

v5.34.0 Wave 5 chunk W5-B1, its one build chunk (#2378 D3). Branch `fix/w5b1-no-login-landing-notice` from `861dbd1f`. Closes #2378. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w5b1-no-login-landing-notice.md` (rev 2, sha256 `22c385ca…31aaf2`; charter v2.21, sha256 `d9a9fe5b…1980`; Architect A176 accepted the plan and the nine-file lock; the notice's copy is Architect A175's text verbatim).

**Why.** W4-B2 made the operator routes that grant or widen authority refuse an operator who is not signed in. On an install with no login (`authStatus: 'open'`), the default one, the page said nothing about it: the dashboard bar's login chip is deliberately silent for `open`, so an operator met each refusal one at a time with no standing explanation.

**What.** A persistent notice on the landing page (`#noLoginBanner`, `role="status"`, after the isolation-override notice) shows exactly when `/api/server-info` reads `authStatus: 'open'`. It says that operator actions here rely on request shape rather than a login, and that the authority-granting actions are refused until a login is enabled and you are signed in. Its **Add a login** button opens Global Settings at "Your account" with the focus there. It is drawn by `renderNoLoginBanner`, called from `renderAuthStatus`, so it mirrors the existing 60 s poll with no dismiss and no timer, and `loadServerInfo` is unchanged: two tests outside the lock run it with every render stubbed by name. It is painted once while it stays up, so a screen reader does not re-announce its `role="status"` text on every poll. It hides for `armed`, `account-required`, `locked`, `unreadable`, `fallback` (Caddy's password is in front of the page and the chip already says what to do) and an absent `authStatus`. The copy is set as text nodes. The chip's behaviour is unchanged. `public/ui.js` is untouched: `#gsAccountSection` already exists, and `openAddLoginSettings` gives it `tabindex=-1` and the focus, as `openInstallOverrideSettings` does for its section.

**Docs.** `docs/user-guide.md` ("Adding a login later") describes the notice, when it shows and when it goes; `docs/configuration-reference.md` ("Routes that need a signed-in operator") says the dashboard notices `authStatus: "open"`.

**Tests.** `test/isolation-override-banner.test.js`: the shipped functions lifted and run: the exact copy, shown for `open` only and hidden for every other `AUTH_STATUSES` value and for none; follows the poll down and back up without appending; a repeat `open` poll leaves the same nodes in place; the chip stays silent for `open` and still warns for `fallback`; text, never markup, and no timer; drawn from `renderAuthStatus`, not `loadServerInfo`; the button opens Global Settings at the account with the focus there, is wired once across polls, and is hidden where there is no Global Settings; shows beside the isolation notice; its slot in `index.html`, hidden, between the isolation and recovery-code notices. `test/signed-in-operator-routes.test.js`: on the same scratch install, `open` serves `authStatus: 'open'` and a class A route refuses `LOGIN_GATE_REQUIRED`; signed in under `armed` it serves `armed` and the same route is not refused by the check. Every existing assertion in both files is unchanged. Narrow run: every test file that reads `public/landing.js` or `public/index.html` plus the three out-of-lock tests, green.

**W4-B2 leftovers, recorded and not fixed here (charter §1.3(h), Architect A175; no new issue).** (a) The global-rules and startup-prompt editors on the landing page still show Save to an operator with no login, who then gets the server's refusal; the notice now explains it. (b) `projectRecoveryControlHtml` (`public/ui.js`) keeps a readback branch that the recovery panel can no longer reach. (c) `checkWatchRoute`'s `options` pass-through (`server.js`) has no caller.

**Critic (cumulative at `9797cd75`, then verify-resolutions on the fix).** 0 blocking.

## 2026-10-17 — #2349, #2438, #1413: the temp-repo guard sees `-C` argv and named git wrappers; a skipped describe is pinned against the real reporter

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w5a1-temp-repo-guard-survivors -->

v5.34.0 Wave 5 chunk W5-A1, its one build chunk (Option B). Branch `fix/w5a1-temp-repo-guard-survivors` from `861dbd1f`. Closes #2349, #1413 and #2438. Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/w5-a1-guard-and-skip-pin-plan.md` (rev 3, sha256 `01440772…ca73`); charter v2.21 (sha256 `d9a9fe5b…1980`) locks ten files after Architect A174 chose Option B.

**Why.** The #831 guard in `test/temp-repo.test.js` exists so that no test reads the machine's git template, which TangleClaw rewrites while the suite runs. It recognised only argv elements that look like options before the subcommand, so `execFileSync('git', ['-C', dir, 'init', '-q'])` passed it, and it could not see a git wrapper with a name other than `git` (`run('init')` in `test/cache-bump-guard.test.js`). Sixteen inits of those two shapes got past it: fifteen direct `-C` sites across six files (#2438) and the one wrapper (#2349). Separately, nothing pinned how node:test's junit reporter emits a skipped `describe` — one testcase at the describe's own path, children unlisted — though the shipped ledger's `caddy-adapt-absent` pattern is anchored on exactly that path (#1413).

**What.** The argv shape accepts the expression `-C` takes (an identifier, member chain, one-level call, or string literal). A per-file pass collects wrappers defined as `NAME = (...args) => exec*('git', <args or an array spreading it>)` and flags only those names called with `init`/`clone`, so a non-git wrapper called with `'init'`, or `mk('clone')` with no git wrapper defined, stays clean. All sixteen sites now call `initRepo` (the wrapper with `['-b', 'main']` and its own `GIT_ENV`). `SANCTIONED` is unchanged. **Not closed by this entry:** a helper that takes the directory first, `function git(cwd, ...args)` called as `git(dir, 'init', …)`, is still invisible to the scanner. The Critic counted 23 such calls in 14 files, outside this chunk's ten-file lock; they are routed to the Integrator for an issue rather than widened here. A new real-producer case in `test/test-skip-audit.test.js` runs a skipped describe and a sibling skipped `it` through `node --test --test-reporter=junit` and audits the report against the shipped ledger; `CI_SKIPS` lists the two paths.

**Evidence.** With the guard extended and nothing migrated, the whole-tree guard failed naming exactly the sixteen planned lines, then went green after migration. Disabling the `-C` alternative, and separately the wrapper pass, turned the new unit case red. A fixture whose describe is not skipped (an inner `it` skipped instead) turned the #1413 case red. Each mutation was restored by its inverse edit to a byte-identical file. The seven migrated files and the two guard/audit files pass alone. No existing assertion changed.

## 2026-10-17 — #2378 (refs): operator routes that grant or approve authority refuse an operator who is not signed in

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=w4b2-signed-in-operator-routes -->

v5.34.0 Wave 4 chunk W4-B2, its one build chunk. Branch `fix/w4b2-signed-in-operator-routes` from `231a555b`. Refs #2378, which stays OPEN (the landing-page notice and a separately tracked finding remain). Plan: `.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w4b2-signed-in-operator-routes-redacted.md` (rev 3 rebound, sha256 `40515e21…df2e4a`; charter v2.15, sha256 `5367d7aa…e81ee32`; Architect A154 accepted the plan and the five-file lock; the direct Operator transcript verification is A147, steps 2486/2526). The lock grew to 22 files under charter v2.18 (sha256 `c0683e84…121c42b`) and addendum A rev 7 (`.tangleclaw/plans/build-plan-w4b2-addendum-a-test-lock.md`, sha256 `e0184a61…51e7`), after Architect A156 (expansion held) and A157 (UI change accepted in principle), formally accepted by Architect A159 (the 20-file lock) and A162 (the 22-file lock): 14 existing test files that acted as the operator by request shape, the recovery panel's offer in `public/ui.js`, one name in `test/launch-readiness-panel.test.js`'s function list, and three sentences of `docs/user-guide.md`.

**Why.** With no login in force (`open`, or `fallback` behind Caddy's password) the operator is recognised by a request's shape (a browser's `Origin`/`Sec-Fetch-Site`, or `x-tangleclaw-client: dashboard`), which any process on the host can send, a project session included. The operator-only routes that grant or approve authority over sessions therefore did not keep a session from granting itself something on a default install.

**What.** A route option, `signedInOperator: { action }`, enforced in the dispatcher right after the launch-binding guard admits the request, and only when it admitted the caller as the operator: a project, the Project Master or a service token never meets it. `_requireSignedInOperator` (generalised from the launch-isolation override's helper) refuses an operator with no signed-in session: `open` → `403 LOGIN_GATE_REQUIRED` with a message naming the action and the way through (turn the login on, sign in, send again); `fallback` → `409 GATE_FALLBACK`. Each refusal is logged at warn with `code`, `method`, `path`, `gateState`. Twenty routes carry the option (session rules' six operator routes, the three global/Master rule routes, learning tier, coordinator role grant, control release and close, merge-queue create and its three operator decisions, the startup prompt, shared-doc registration, adding a group member). `PATCH /api/projects/:name` asks in its handler when the body names `launchSequence.recoveryMode`, whichever branch admitted it: a rename that also names the mode is refused too. `controlRoute`, `mergeQueueRoute` and `checkWatchRoute` pass registration options through. The two launch-isolation override PUTs move onto the option with their own refusal, kept byte-identical (status, code, message and log) and answered 403 in `fallback` as before, a bounded exception ruled KEEP by the Integrator and confirmed by Architect A155.

**The page.** The recovery panel no longer offers the recovery-mode choice with no login in force: a new `recoveryModeChoiceOffer` (`public/ui.js`) wraps the shared `recoveryModeControlOffer` and shows why instead (turn the login on, sign in). The shared function's code is unchanged, because the launch-isolation override switch and the refused-launch message read it too, and turning an override off still needs no sign-in. Its doc comment, which said the server took the dashboard's request there, is corrected. `docs/user-guide.md` no longer says the startup prompt and the recovery mode can be changed with no login.

**Existing tests (14 files).** Their calls to the class A routes are now made by a signed-in operator: each suite that calls one turns the login on and signs in (the existing `signInOperator`/`operatorOf` helpers), or, where a suite shares its server, turns it on for that suite only and back off after. Their assertions are unchanged except these, each because the behaviour it pinned is the one this change removes: the recovery-mode save on an open or fallback install is refused (403/409, nothing recorded) where it succeeded; an open-install prompt write is refused `LOGIN_GATE_REQUIRED` where it was `OPEN_INSTALL_TOKEN_INVALID`; and the proof recorded by these routes is the signed-in one (`operator-verified`, `verified-session`) where it was the open-install one (`open-install-unverified`, `ambient-open`), in `api-projects`, `project-recovery-mode-control`, `merge-queue-api` and `startup-prompt-api`. A decision recorded on an open install before this change still reads `open-install-unverified` (seeded through the store). Two new cases assert the open-install refusal of the recovery mode.

**Tests.** New `test/signed-in-operator-routes.test.js`: every class A route refused under `open` (both shapes, 403) and `fallback` (both shapes, 409), with the whole store (every table, the global rules, the scratch home's files) unchanged; the same requests succeed for a signed-in operator under `armed`; a valid binding plus a browser shape is refused; a session's own proposal, an authority's own release and a port lease are unchanged; the narrowing direction (override off, role revoke, HOLD, STOP, member removal, an ordinary setting, a rename) still needs no sign-in. `test/launch-binding-routes.test.js` gains a `describe` that `deepEqual`s the route table's marked routes to a reviewed list.

**Critic (cumulative, at `ba27cc06`).** 0 blocking.

**rev-20261017T030512Z-9340fa32** — scope `w4b2-signed-in-operator-routes`, 2026-10-17T03:07:47Z

Prune with the D3 landing-page chunk (owner TC-Lane-B). |
