On the launch screen the refusal is a six-second toast (#2339, open), so the sentence is in practice read in the launch's answer and the guides. With both overrides on, the records say `install`. Matching a pane to its launch costs one more tmux read for each pane that runs that command, and none for any other. `tc sessions isolation` still tells the operator to relaunch such a session; that wording is TC-Lane-A's piece.

**Browser check.** Checked by an automated browser run on `7eac34fe`, before the mark was bound to the pane's launch id; not yet seen by the Operator (VRF-007). The refusal with its new sentence, 1055 characters, read whole at desktop, 375 and 320; at 320 one word of the reason, the executable's path, clips (#2339). From the browser with no login in force the set route answered `403 LOGIN_GATE_REQUIRED`; with the install-wide override set through the store accessor in the scratch home, `POST /api/sessions/:project` answered `201` with `isolationOverride`, and the pane's command, the log warning, the activity event and the inventory row agreed. Record and captures: `.prawduct/lane-evidence/2229-c01b/browser/` in TC-Lane-B's clone.

**Tests.** New `test/launch-isolation-override-launch.test.js`, through the real launch and 01a's accessor on a scratch store: refused with no override; the project's override starts that project only, on the exact executable with its mode's flags and no isolation flag, with the warning, the event and the answer field; the install-wide one starts any project; `version_unknown` is covered and recorded as not read; a wrapper, an unnameable path, a builder or judge fault, a refusal not marked overridable and each hidden refusal stay refused; seven seam answers that are not `allowed: true`, an unreadable table, a throwing adapter and a missing one each start nothing; a verified version never asks; the field through `POST /api/sessions/:project`, that launch's inventory row and the line `tc` prints, beside it a hand-made session with the same command reading unproved, and that launch's own tmux session replaced under the same name, its event still present, reading unproved. Cases added to `test/startup-control-codex.test.js`, `test/codex-launch-modes.test.js`, `test/launch-isolation-inventory.test.js`, `test/launch-isolation-route.test.js`, `test/master.test.js` and `test/startup-control-launch.test.js`. No existing assertion was edited. Within this branch, the inventory cases first written for a mark with null detail were rewritten to require the unproved row, after the Integrator's `5a12c29f` accepted that shape.

## 2026-10-12 — #2229: the operator's launch-isolation overrides are stored, and only the operator can read, set or clear them

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2229-01a-isolation-override-store -->

#2229 Chunk 01a: the store and the routes of the override. Branch `feat/2229-01a-isolation-override-store`, from `origin/main` `1d6a72ed`. Built to the decider's rulings (queue Rulings items 10, 58 and 59). **No launch reads the setting in this chunk:** the launch path (chunk 01b) and the screens (chunk 02) are TC-Lane-B's and follow; all are merged before the one deploy, so no install serves these routes alone. That is a bounded exception to "a setting must take effect, or say why it does not", by the Integrator's word.

Visual change: no.

**Why.** Since #2246 a Codex that cannot be shown to be kept off its shared background process is refused. The Operator ruled that refusal stays the default, with an override per project or per install. An override a session could set for itself would defeat the refusal, and so would one kept in `config.json` or `project.json`: `PATCH /api/config` asks nobody who they are, and a project's agent can write its own files.

**What changed.**

- `lib/store.js`, schema v66: table `launch_isolation_overrides`. A row is an override that is on; no row is off. `scope` is `install` or `project`; a CHECK ties the install scope to having no project; two partial unique indexes make exactly one install row and one row per project properties of the database (a nullable `project_id` alone would admit several install rows, since SQLite counts NULLs as distinct). The DDL is shared by `_createTables` and the migration, and the postcondition reads it back before the version advances. `store.launchIsolationOverrides`: `list`, `set`, `clear` (each idempotent; a change writes `launch.isolation_override_set` or `_cleared` to the activity log in the same transaction) and `effectiveFor(projectId)`, the one question the launch path will ask: install-wide or the project's, `null` for the Project Master (install-wide only), never throws, and anything it cannot read is not allowed, with the reason `unreadable`. `projects.delete` removes the project's row.
- `server.js`: `GET /api/launch/isolation-overrides`, `PUT` and `DELETE` of `/install` and of `/projects/:id`. Each opens with `operatorProjectCaller`, before any lookup; the read guard of `GET /api/launch/isolation`, which admits the Master and verified sessions, is not copied. **Turning an override on also needs a signed-in operator:** on an install with no login in force, `operatorProjectCaller` admits any request shaped like a browser's or carrying the dashboard's marker, and a session could send one and grant itself the override (found by the review, confirmed by a run, ruled 'open refuse'). Both `PUT` routes refuse a request with no sign-in, `403 LOGIN_GATE_REQUIRED`, changing nothing and saying to turn the login on. Reading and turning off stay open to the operator's shape: off is the safe direction.
- `server.js`, for chunk 01b: `POST /api/sessions/:project`'s 201 passes through `isolationOverride` from the launch result, `null` today. No launch sets it in this chunk.
- Docs: `docs/configuration-reference.md`.

**Rollback.** Purely additive. An older server on a v66 store runs no migration and ignores the table, so every override stops applying and such a launch is refused as before. By hand: `DROP TABLE launch_isolation_overrides`.

**Limits.** The other operator-only routes still accept the request shape on an install with no login (#2378). An unidentified caller's set or clear is refused by the launch binding guard (`403 LAUNCH_BINDING_REQUIRED`, or the login gate's `401`) before the handler, so it does not read `OPERATOR_ONLY` there; the Integrator accepted that ('2229 guard code ok'). The routes' answers do not carry the sentence about what an override risks: that text is chunk 01b's. Which refusals an override may cover (`version_unknown`, `version_unverified`) is decided by the caller of `effectiveFor`, not by the store. The plan said the table would be described in the reference's SQLite section; that section lists no tables by its own rule, so the storage is described with the routes.

**Tests.** New `test/store-isolation-override-migration.test.js`: fresh and migrated shape, four wrong-shaped tables that stop the version advancing, and what the database itself refuses, each asked of a fresh store and of one migrated from v65, with a second install write through the accessor leaving one row. New `test/launch-isolation-override.test.js`: the accessor, `effectiveFor` failing closed on a missing table, a row of the wrong shape and a closed store, the five routes for a signed-in operator, with the name recorded; on an install with no login, a set refused for the dashboard's marker alone, a `Sec-Fetch-Site` header alone and a whole browser shape, each with no row changed, and the read and clear still answered; each route refused for a project session, the Project Master, an unidentified caller and an ended session, with what is stored compared before and after; the launch answer's `isolationOverride`, `null` and passed through. `test/launch-binding-routes.test.js`: the four state-changing routes, none reachable unidentified. Deliberate breaks of the new code, each caught: the output is in the lane's evidence folder.

## 2026-10-12 — #2222: a Codex version refusal says how to stop the update prompt moving Codex off a verified version

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=codex-isolation-2222 -->

#2222 Chunk 02, the issue's only chunk in v5.33. Branch `feat/2222-02-update-prompt-advice`, from `origin/main` `fa029c0d`, with `d5c5a491` merged in. Chunk 01 (admitting newer Codex versions) is not built in this release, by ruling; the plan is not complete and #2222 stays open for v5.34. **No Codex version is admitted and no list changes.**

Visual change: yes — the launch-failed banner carries a longer text.

**Why.** A version refusal tells the operator to install a verified Codex. A verified Codex that knows of a newer release opens on a prompt whose default choice (Enter) runs `npm install -g @openai/codex`, the newest version, which TangleClaw refuses: the operator is back where they started.

**What was measured.** On 0.156.1 and on 0.157.1, each alone, as `codex` and as `codex --no-daemon`, a fake key, no account, a throwaway home whose `version.json` named a newer version: with `check_for_update_on_startup = true` in Codex's `config.toml` the update prompt was the first screen; with `false`, the home otherwise the same, it was not drawn.

**What changed.**

- `lib/startup-control-codex.js`: `_recoveryFor` adds one passage to the two remedies that name a version to install (`version_unknown` / `version_unverified`, and `native_version_unverified` when it names versions): what the prompt's default does, the setting, the file, the versions it was measured on, and that TangleClaw does not edit that file. The third wording ("no Codex version fixes this") is left alone: staying on a version fixes nothing there. New `_updatePromptAdvice` returns the passage only when every version the remedy names is in `UPDATE_PROMPT_MEASURED_ON`, and names only those versions, so a version listed later without this measurement gets no advice and a remedy never mentions a version its own path refuses.
- `data/engines/codex.json`: the `update` guarded dialog's evidence records the measurement and what was not measured. No pattern or list changed.
- Docs: `docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`.

**Limits.** The passage is about the prompt. Not measured: an update made with no prompt; anything after sign-in; whether `false` also stops the check itself. The launch-failed banner is a toast that leaves after six seconds (#2339, open): the text, now longer, cannot in practice be read there in time; it is also in the API's answer and both guides. At 320 px a long executable path in the reason clips (#2339).

**Tests.** `test/startup-control-codex.test.js`: the passage on both version refusals (prompt, setting, file, no claim of a silent update, every named version measured, placed before the closing lines); on the native path, present for a measured version and naming it alone, absent when a named version was not measured, absent when no version is the remedy. No existing assertion was edited.

**Browser check.** Checked by an automated browser run; not yet seen by the Operator. A scratch server on loopback behind a tailnet-only forwarder, a fake `codex` reporting 0.161.0: the whole text read at desktop and 375; at 320 as above.

## 2026-10-12 — #2228: tc tells a session when its pane was not started isolated, and never calls it isolated

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=codex-told-2228 -->

#2228 Chunk 01, the issue's only chunk. Branch `feat/2228-tc-told-not-isolated`, from `origin/main` `f0020c4d`. Built to the decider's rulings (queue Rulings items 11, 13 and 56): no unbound lookup, no positive from the environment or from a binding, the existing judgment loaded on demand, the state step's row printed by `tc`.

Visual change: no

**Why.** A Codex session started on Codex's shared background process may send `tc` calls under another launch's identity. The operator was told (#2222, #2233); the session was not. The issue's premise (a launch that warns and starts anyway) is not in the tree, since an unisolated launch is refused, so the sessions this reaches are those started before that check and still running.

**What changed.**

- `lib/tc-verbs.js`: `tc whoami`, `tc capabilities` and the state step of `tc start next` / `tc start review` print a `Launch isolation:` row.
  - `readPaneStartCommand` reads `#{pane_start_command}` of the pane `TMUX_PANE` names; `judgeOwnPaneCommand` takes the PATH floor and the credential handoff off it and hands it to `lib/startup-control-codex.js#judgeRecordedCommand`. The answer is `not-isolated`, `not-shown` or `not-applicable`. A command that reads isolated, one attached with `--remote`, one that names Codex in a form not judged, an unreadable one and no pane at all are all `not-shown`. There is no positive.
  - `renderServerIsolationRecord`: with a verified binding and a local reading that applies, one aux `GET /api/launch/isolation`; the row is chosen by the binding's verified session id (the Master's row for a verified Master) and printed as the server's record, lower case, ending "That does not show this shell is that launch." Not asked for an unverified binding; `unavailable` on a failed lookup; an answer in an unknown shape reads as no record.
  - The state step: the row is printed above the page by `tc`, in brackets, marked as read now and no part of the step. The served content and its digest are untouched. It takes only the room the page's own decorations leave of `lib/launch-page.js#pageOverhead`, so a full page still fits the engine's limit; cut, it keeps the verdict and ends "… Run tc whoami for the rest." On `tc start review` the ordinary notice is cut. A `not-applicable` reading prints nothing there, and no failure of the notice costs the page.
  - `_requireQuietly`: the Codex module reaches `lib/store.js`, and node prints its SQLite `ExperimentalWarning` when that loads. That one warning is dropped for the length of the load; every other passes; `emitWarning` is restored on a throw. `tc` opens no database. `_withoutPathFloor` repeats `lib/sessions.js`'s pattern, which `tc` cannot load; a test holds the two to the same answers. Both go away with #2375.
- `bin/tc`: hands the verbs a reader of the pane's start command, called only by the verbs that print the row.
- `docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`: the row, what each answer means to the session reading it, the server's line, and the limits.

**Tests.** `test/tc-pane-identity.test.js`: each command shape, wrapped and malformed, fails closed; a borrowed live verified binding whose row is isolated leaves the caller NOT SHOWN ISOLATED; the row is never picked by a claimed id; no output holds `ISOLATED` as the caller's verdict over every reading, binding and server answer; `next` and `review` both carry the notice and only on the state step; a full state page with the longest reasons fits the limit; the warning filter; three cases through the real binary. `test/tc-start-cli.test.js`: one stub keeps the first url asked, because the state step now makes a side lookup after it.

**Not covered.** A real Codex pane on the live install (after the deploy). A session that never runs `tc` is still not told. No route was added and no guard changed; `server.js` is not in the diff, and no route-table test was added.

## 2026-10-12 — #2059: Codex 0.161.0 and 0.162.0 stay unlisted, and the streaming frame that keeps them out is pinned

<!-- prawduct: type=chore | scope=codex-wake-2059 -->

#2059 Chunk 02b, the issue's last chunk in v5.33. Branch `feat/2059-02b-codex-streaming-known-unsafe`, from `origin/main` `355a946b`, with `f0020c4d` merged in. The issue is an outside contributor's and stays open, for v5.34. **No product code changes and no Codex version is added to `verifiedVersions`:** nothing here changes what an operator sees.

Visual change: no

**Why.** Each version was run signed in, in its own run, through a scratch TangleClaw whose own copy of the profile listed that one version (the tracked profile was never edited). The launch's automatic fire applied on the default and on a three-item status line; a folder with no trust entry was refused; the update prompt was held and named; and with real mail waiting the wake held on `engine-thread-busy` at every tick of a real streaming turn, including over a pane held still mid-answer. Then the case that decides it, asked for by the Architect and the decider: with the pane's TUI process stopped mid-answer and the turn then ending, the thread read idle under a pane still showing the answer cut off mid-line, the wake read that pane as `pane-at-prompt`, and it typed its nudge into it, on both versions (0.161.0: 15.6 s after the thread read idle, 38 s before the pane was redrawn; 0.162.0: 16.5 s and 37 s). Ruled by the decider on its own read of the recordings: both versions stay unlisted on the wake as merged, each measured streaming frame goes on the known-unsafe list, the list's guard is kept, the limit is stated.

**What changed.**

- `test/fixtures/codex-panes/codex-0.161.0.json`, `codex-0.162.0.json`: one pane added to each set's `panes`, `streamingDefaultLayout`: the frame that version's pane showed for the whole time its renderer was stopped (an empty composer with the cursor on it, the status row and the footer below, the answer cut mid-line above, no busy row), 80 columns by 24 rows, each version's own capture, labelled `remote-channel` (in 0.161.0's file the rest of `panes` is the other launch path). Read without styling, and the pane's note says so; only the scratch project path was replaced. Each file's note says which launch path each pane is and the new pane's size.
- `test/medusa-wake-codex-fixtures.test.js`: `KNOWN_UNSAFE_IF_CHANNEL_IDLE` gains that pane for 0.161.0 and for 0.162.0, and its comment and its exact-contents test say the list now holds two kinds of hole. New, per version from its own pane: the pane is a channel capture drawn like rest; the pane gate alone calls it at the prompt once it has been still; a busy answer over it holds and types nothing; and the measured hole itself, the answer going from busy to idle over the one still frame, ends in a nudge after the settling tick. That last test is green today because the hole is open; a gate that closes the hole turns it red, which is the signal to take the pane off the list and pin its hold instead. The guard that no listed version is one the profile verifies is unedited, and now covers both versions.
- `test/startup-control-codex.test.js`: a channel row holding both a recorded and a provisional thread is refused by the gate, both fires and the wake's observation on every look, nothing is sent, and no reader rewrites the row. Pinned as a state; no path is known to write it.
- `data/engines/codex.json`: evidence text only (why neither version is listed; that a streaming answer draws no busy row). No pattern, `verifiedVersions` and `remoteTrustPrompt.absentOn` untouched.
- Docs: `docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`.

**What remains, for v5.34.** A gate that refuses a pane which has not been redrawn since the turn ended, on positive evidence that it was redrawn (the absence of a busy row is not that evidence), for longer than the settling interval and with mail waiting; that still nudges a session at rest as promptly as now; that keeps holding a running turn on the thread's answer; proved signed in through a whole launch and wake for each version separately. Then that version's pane leaves the list for that reason, and the list's guard changes only with the Architect's acceptance.

**Limits of the evidence.** Every still frame was made by stopping the renderer, not by a pause in the model; no natural pause longer than a wake tick was obtained, so how often a real session shows one is unknown. Nothing was typed while a thread read active. A resume was not run through a server (TangleClaw's launch does not start one). In a folder with no trust entry the startup fire refuses and the wake still nudged, on both versions: observed, not judged here.

**Tests.** `test/medusa-wake-codex-fixtures.test.js`, `test/startup-control-codex.test.js`. One existing expectation changed, the list's exact contents; no other assertion was edited. `test/_wake-fixtures.js` and `test/_codex-visible-pane-fixtures.js` are unedited.

**For the record.** The Chunk 03 entry below ("a native Codex launch is gated by its idle thread and its pane") does not name by path every file its merge changed. Read from that merge's own file list: besides the three the Chunk 02a entry already named (`docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`, `data/engines/codex.json`), it changed `test/_codex-visible-pane-fixtures.js`, `test/launch-bootstrap.test.js`, `test/pane-witness.test.js`, `test/startup-control-codex.test.js` and `test/startup-control.test.js`.

## 2026-10-12 — #2323: a watched check wakes its project, and each message's delivery is read back

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2323-check-watch-wake -->

#2323 Chunk 03b, the issue's last. Branch `feat/2323-03b-check-watch-wake`, from `origin/main` `355a946b`. Built to the ruled contract `2323 relax` (queue Rulings item 31): one marker per watch and message, ONE second send after two unbroken connected minutes, never a third; a duplicate is possible and is said. The issue stays open for its live check after the deploy.

Visual change: no

**Why.** A watch's result was recorded and nobody was told: a session had to read its watch. The issue asks that the session be woken when the check finishes or the due time passes, that the delivery be read back from the switchboard's own facts, and that it survive a restart of the server and a clear of the session.

**What changed.**

- `lib/check-watch.js`: after its reads, each pass sends what is owed, as a switchboard participant of its own (`check-watch`, shown as *Check Watch*), through `medusa-send#sendTracked` with `caller: { kind: 'system' }`. It declares itself the system owner of the request id prefix `check-watch:`.
  - A **wake** when a result is recorded and an **alarm** when the due time passes with none, each under `check-watch:<kind>:<watch id>`. The message ends `ref: <request id>`.
  - `noteArrival`: a received message binds a Hub id to an exchange only by that marker, and only when the exchange is the monitor's, the message came from the workspace it was sent from and reached the workspace it was addressed to. The measurement showed the arrival reaches the observer before the Hub's answer on every send, so this is the ordinary path and the answer's own bind then changes nothing.
  - The recipient is the project's one active session with a connected listener. None, several, or one not connected records `wake-undeliverable` / `alarm-undeliverable` once, spends no request id, and the message is sent when there is one.
  - `woke` / `alarmed` is recorded once when the send is made, with a code for how it stood then (`accepted`, `uncertain`, `refused`). A send the Hub refused is final.
  - **The second attempt:** a first send with no Hub id and no refusal is sent once more under `...:2` when its recipient's listener has been connected without a break for two minutes, counted from the later of when the outcome became uncertain and when that listener last connected. Found from `medusa-exchanges#openSystemOwned`, so it survives a restart with no new record. Never a third.
  - An alarm sent more than two minutes after its due time says it is late and names both times; for an expired watch it names the expiry. A second attempt of an alarm is called late only if the first was, and is not made once the watch has its result.
  - A delivered message nobody acknowledged has its exchange closed after 24 hours (a test shows a read and an acknowledgement made after the close are still recorded). A send with no Hub id is left open.
  - **Its listener runs only while it has somebody to tell** (the Integrator's `2323 listen on work`): a watch that is open or owes a message for a project with a connected session, or a first send awaiting its second attempt. `lib/system-health.js` reads the listener count, and a listener that always ran would turn an install with no Hub from clear to fired.
  - A pass waits 15 seconds for the Hub's answer to a send and at most 30 first sends are made in a pass. Carried from chunk 03a's review: the clock is read inside the guarded pass; a skipped pass is logged on a change of state.
- `lib/medusa-listener.js`, `lib/medusa.js` (the Integrator's `2323 since A`, their own commit): a listener keeps `listeningSince`, the time it last reached `listening`, null in every other state, and its status carries it. **Three handlers answer with that status as it is, each under two prefixes, so six route answers gain the key:** `GET <prefix>/status`, `POST <prefix>/toggle`, `POST <prefix>/read`, under `/api/sessions/:project/medusa` and `/api/master/medusa`.
- `server.js`: one call in the arrival observer, after the arrival is recorded, handing the message to `checkWatch.noteArrival`; the comment at the monitor's start.
- `lib/check-watch-api.js`: `MONITORED` is `true`. Every watch in an answer carries `delivery.wake` and `delivery.alarm`: a state (`not-owed`, `owed`, `undeliverable`, `refused`, `unconfirmed`, `accepted`, `arrived`, `read`, `acknowledged`), each attempt, whether there was a second, and a recorded "nobody to tell". Derived from the exchanges' facts on every read; `read` and `acknowledged` count only from the recipient itself.
- `lib/tc-verbs.js`: the NOT MONITORED line is gone. `tc check show` prints one line for each message above the history (`wake: arrived`, `alarm: not-owed`, with `second attempt made`, and the reason while it is `undeliverable`); the Integrator's `2323 show b`.
- `docs/check-watch.md`: the two messages, the marker, the second attempt, the delivery states and the limits.

**Limits, all on the page.** A duplicate message is possible, and is certain in one case: the message arrived, the server stopped before the bind, and the Hub does not deliver it again. A wake can be lost if neither attempt was taken or delivered, or if the session the first send went to has ended (the second attempt goes only to the listener the first was addressed to). The Hub takes a sender as stated, so the marker holds against this install's sessions and not against something that talks to the Hub directly. A Hub that restarts loses what it holds: read from its code, not measured. A refusal is final (#2370 is one way to get one wrongly). Up to a pass late, two when *Check Watch* had to be started. `tc check show` prints each message's state and not each attempt's.

**Not exercised.** No real Hub, no real session and no real GitHub check: the Hub is stood in for at `lib/medusa.js`'s wire calls, with the tracked send, the exchange rows, the bind and (in the API tests) this server's arrival observer the real ones. The live check after the deploy is the Integrator's: one real watch on a real PR's `test` check, woken when it finishes, the delivery read back.

## 2026-10-12 — #2059: Codex 0.161.0 and 0.162.0 are read by the rows each one draws, and by the thread that is the launch's own

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=codex-wake-2059 -->

#2059 Chunk 02a. Branch `feat/2059-02a-codex-per-version-rest-rule`, from `origin/main` `a9b83cd1`, with `2a4c3316` merged in. The issue is an outside contributor's and stays open. **No Codex version is added to `verifiedVersions`:** no launch gets a channel on 0.161.0 or 0.162.0, so nothing here changes what an operator sees until Chunk 02b lists one.

Visual change: no

**Why.** Measured signed in over the launch path TangleClaw uses, each version from its own runs: both draw a footer row (`  ? for shortcuts`) beneath the status row at rest, and that footer ALONE on the screen they open and resume with, under an empty composer that holds the cursor; on a resume the thread already reads idle while that screen is up. So the one-row rule built for 0.156.1 refused every usable composer of theirs (a listed version would always need the Fire by hand), and nothing refused their opening screen once a channel answered idle (no header row names a model). After a first turn both also load a second, ephemeral thread in the launch's folder to name the session, which made an unrecorded launch ambiguous for good.

**What changed.**

- `data/engines/codex.json`: `capabilities.wake.restRows`, a table keyed by exact version, with an entry for `0.161.0` and one for `0.162.0`, each holding `footerPattern` (`^  \? for shortcuts$`) and its own evidence. Nothing for 0.156.1. `verifiedVersions` and `remoteTrustPrompt.absentOn` are untouched.
- `lib/medusa-wake.js`: `restRows` joins `WAKE_FIELDS` (optional). `_isRestRows` refuses a key that is not one exact version, an entry holding anything but `footerPattern` and `evidence`, an entry with no dated evidence, and a pattern that is not anchored at both ends, holds a `|` or matches the empty string; one bad entry refuses the block. Compiled per version and frozen. `_footerOpening` refuses a pane as `engine-starting` when the declared footer is the first row drawn below the last bare composer row, for the exact version passed in and for no other; `_assessPane` and `assessSessionIdle` take that version (`engineVersion`). `_engineActivity` keeps the `version` an answer names and the tick hands it to the pane gate only with a fresh idle. The meaning of `pane-engine-starting` covers both producers; no new reason code.
- `lib/pane-witness.js`: one new option, `footerRe`. Given, the rows below the composer must be exactly a status row and then that footer: the footer first is refused as a screen still opening or resuming (asked before the status pattern is, so a loose status pattern cannot pass it), and every other count or order is refused. Not given, the function is what it was.
- `lib/startup-control-codex.js`:
  - `_provedVersion` and `_restScreen`: the version is the one the launch recorded and the one its app-server reports, equal; never the installed binary's. Unproved, the pane proof is refused, lasting (`version-unproved`, worded with both values). Proved with no entry, the witness is asked exactly what it was asked before (`statusRe: STATUS_RE, maxStatusRows: 1`). Proved with an entry: `footerRe`, and a status pattern that also takes the launch thread's own model alone on its row, without case (`_statusRowPattern`); never `maxStatusRows: 2`. The model is the one read before the pane, and `_paneAndThread` refuses if the thread reports another afterwards.
  - `_threadKind`, on those versions only: `launch` (`threadSource: "user"`, not ephemeral, a rollout path, no parent), `title` (`thread_title`, ephemeral, `path: null`), else `unidentified`. `_launchThread` and `observeActivity` bind an unbound channel only to the sole `launch` thread; the title helper never binds and never makes two; an unidentified or unreadable thread refuses; another thread working or in an unknown status now holds a fire as well as the wake. A recorded thread stays authoritative. The re-list after the compare-and-set requires the same sole `launch` thread.
  - **The binding is an inference until it is proved** (`_proveLaunchThread`, used by the gate, both fires and `observeActivity` on those versions; the Architect held two heads of this PR on it, and the ProjectManager one). A compare-and-set on the launch's own open channel, with no thread of either kind on it, writes the thread to its own field, `adapterState.provisionalThreadId`, and leaves `adapterState.threadId` empty, so no reader of the recorded thread (this module's, a fire's reconcile, a managed rotation's) can take an unproved id for one. A write that throws, answers nothing, or that this look did not itself make (`written !== true`, even with the same thread then on the row) binds nothing and sends nothing. The threads are listed and read again and must be the same set with the same sole `launch` thread; a second guarded write, also this look's own, moves the id to `threadId` and clears the provisional field. A check that fails after the first write withdraws the inference by a guarded write (that thread, still the provisional one) and refuses whether or not the withdrawal lands; the next look proves a provisional thread from the first list again or withdraws it. The channel row is read again before any answer (open, same launch, same thread, no provisional thread beside it), for a recorded thread too. `_paneAndThread`, given the channel on those versions, asks that whole question again after the pane is witnessed (new refusal `at: 'threads'`, worded with the reader's own reason) in place of re-reading the one thread. 0.156.1's bind and its single re-read are unchanged.
  - `observeActivity` names the proved `version` on its answers, only where that version has an entry.
- `lib/startup-control.js`: passes a non-empty string `version` through.
- Fixtures: `test/fixtures/codex-panes/codex-0.162.0.json` (new) and `channelPanes`, `turnFrames` and `threads` added to `codex-0.161.0.json`, whose older `panes` are now labelled as the other launch path (a plain launch on the shared daemon, footer `  ← for agents · ? for shortcuts`). Whole reads with the cursor, and whole `thread/read` answers of the launch's thread and the title helper; scratch paths and thread ids replaced. `test/_codex-visible-pane-fixtures.js` loads them; `test/_wake-fixtures.js` gains the one line that loads the 0.162.0 set.
- Docs: `docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`.

**Limits, each measured and pinned.** While a turn's answer streams, both versions draw no busy row: an empty composer, the status row and the footer, as at rest. The witness on one read cannot tell that frame from rest; what refuses it is the thread read after the pane, the two-read witness when the transcript moved, and the wake's `engine-thread-busy`. An empty status line at rest is one row, the footer with a context item beside it: the launch witness refuses it (hand Fire), the wake's rule does not. A single-item status line is recognised only when the item is the model.

**Departure from the plan, agreed by the Integrator before the build.** The plan refuses any proved version that is neither 0.156.1 nor declared, lasting. As built, a proved version with no `restRows` entry keeps the one-row rule, under which a screen that draws a footer is refused anyway: telling 0.156.1 from "any other" would need a table entry for 0.156.1, which the plan excludes. Not reachable today, since only 0.156.1 is a verified version. An unproved version is refused, lasting, as planned. `test/_wake-fixtures.js` is outside the plan's file list; its one line was added by the Integrator's leave.

**Tests.** `test/pane-witness.test.js`, `test/startup-control-codex.test.js`, `test/startup-control.test.js`, `test/medusa-wake-codex-fixtures.test.js`, `test/medusa-wake-engine-activity.test.js`, `test/medusa-wake.test.js`, `test/engine-wake-profiles.test.js`. No assertion about 0.156.1 was changed; two existing tests were extended for the new field (the profile-derivation loop, and the fixture matrix's tables and its idle answer, which names the version for a set of a declared one). `KNOWN_UNSAFE_IF_CHANNEL_IDLE` loses 0.161.0's opening screen because the gate now refuses it under 0.161.0's declaration; 0.159.0 stays. Each new rule was broken in turn and a test of this chunk went red (`.prawduct/lane-evidence/2059-c02a/break-rules-run.txt`, not tracked).

**Not covered.** Nothing was run through a scratch server or the facade's version gate: an automatic fire applied on a real session of either version is Chunk 02b's sitting. A streaming turn that pauses with nothing moving; a turn that runs a command or asks for approval; a status line that wraps; a pane narrower than 100 columns. Whether 0.156.1 loads a title thread was not measured, and its rule is unchanged.

**For the record.** The Chunk 03 entry below does not name `docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/user-guide.md` and `data/engines/codex.json`, which that chunk also changed.

## 2026-10-12 — #2320: a coordinated project's wrap leaves the shared priming file alone

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=2320-lane-wrap-no-auto-merge -->

#2320 Chunk 02, the issue's last. Branch `feat/2320-02-priming-roll-skipped`, from `origin/main` `a9b83cd1`. The issue stays open until one real lane wrap is read back on the live install after the deploy.

Visual change: yes (the wrap drawer's release banner has a new label and sentence for a coordinated project's open wrap PR; its priming and commit rows show new text)

**Why.** A lane Builder's wrap rewrote the *Current build chunk* block of `.tangleclaw/priming/build-session.md`, a tracked file every checkout of the repository shares, from the one unapproved plan its own clone held, and carried the rewrite in its wrap commit (#2319).

**Root cause.** The priming roll took the plan it found in the checkout as the project's plan. In a project that works under a coordinator that plan is one lane's, and nothing asked whose project it was.

**What changed.**

- `lib/wrap-steps/priming-roll.js`: the step asks `lib/merge-authority.js` before it resolves any plan. Any answer but `self` is a `skipped` result that stages nothing, so the commit step has nothing to write: the reason opens with the coordinator the settings name (or says the project is the coordinator itself), or says what could not be read. It opens with the name because the wrap drawer cuts a row's reason to one line. It is asked with no repository first, so a project that names no coordinator runs no git command and rolls exactly as before. Otherwise the step reads `git remote get-url origin` and the current branch (`git symbolic-ref`, so a detached HEAD is no branch) and asks again with both, the two the commit step asks with on a protected branch (on another branch the `pr-merge` row is keyed on each pull request's base instead, so the two rows can word the reason differently); a read that fails gives `unknown`, which also stages nothing. Being asked first also means plans that would block the step (several in progress) do not block a coordinated project's wrap.
- `public/wrap-drawer.js`: once GitHub reports the wrap's PR as pending, the drawer's banner read "release pending checks … it lands when its checks pass" for every unarmed PR. For a PR left unarmed because the project's merges are not its own, that was false: nothing lands it until it is merged by hand. `wrapPrInfo` now carries the commit step's `mergeAuthority` when there is one, and `prOutcomeBanner`'s unarmed pending branch then reads "Wrap committed — PR open, waiting to be merged" with the reason. Every other banner is unchanged, and a wrap with no `mergeAuthority` reads as before. Found by the browser check; `public/wrap-drawer.js` is network-first in `public/sw.js`, so no cache name moves (`scripts/cache-bump-guard.js`).
- `docs/user-guide.md`, "Wrap commits and protected branches": the skipped roll and where its reason is shown; "Did it actually ship?": the new banner. `docs/configuration-reference.md`, the `workloadNudge` row: naming a coordinator also stops the roll.

**Tests.** `test/wrap-step-priming-roll.test.js` gains the lane cases, including one through the step's real git reads on a real repository; its existing cases run unedited. New `test/wrap-pipeline-lane.test.js` drives the real pipeline runner with the real priming, commit, PR-gate and PR-merge steps on a real repository whose pushes land in a local bare one, `gh` answered in the test: a project naming a coordinator keeps its priming file byte-identical and arms nothing at either arming site, with both reasons read off the rows the wrap drawer builds; the same project naming nobody rolls the file and arms, at both sites.

`test/wrap-drawer.test.js`: the banner's lane wording, every other outcome compared byte for byte with and without a merge authority, and the path from the wrap's result to the banner.

**Limits.** No real wrap of a lane session has run: that is the Operator's to allow after the deploy, and the hold on lane wraps stays until then. The commit row still reads "wrap PR opened (auto-merge NOT armed)" with its reason under *How to fix this*, and the drawer's tooltip for the priming step does not list this skip (#2366). The banner shown before GitHub's answer arrives ("release pending PR merge") is unchanged. A coordinator's own wrap skips the roll too.

## 2026-10-12 — #2320: a wrap arms auto-merge only in a project that decides its own merges

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=2320-lane-wrap-no-auto-merge -->

#2320 Chunk 01. Branch `feat/2320-01-merge-authority`, from `origin/main` `18644814`. The issue stays open: chunk 02 (the priming roll) is its last.

Visual change: no

**Why.** A wrap of a lane Builder opened a pull request to `main` and armed squash auto-merge on it by itself (#2319), in a project whose merges belong to its Integrator. The Operator's scope note on the issue puts the fix where merge authority is decided, for every caller that can arm, failing closed.

**Root cause.** Arming was decided by where the wrap ran (a protected branch, a pull request resolved as merge) and never by whose decision the merge is. No record was consulted.

**What changed.**

- New `lib/merge-authority.js`. `forProject(project, { repo, baseBranch })` answers `self`, `integrator` or `unknown`, and never throws. The record is the project's own `workloadNudge.coordinatorProject` setting: naming a coordinator hands the merges to it. Where the repository and base have an active merge queue, its owner must be that coordinator or the project itself; another owner is `unknown`. Settings that cannot be read, a value that is not a project name, a repository that cannot be named and a queue record that cannot be read are all `unknown`. `self` is the answer only when the settings read cleanly and name nobody, and then the queue is not read at all, so a project with no coordinator wraps as before on a repository that has a queue.
- `lib/wrap-steps/commit.js`: the close-loop asks before it opens the wrap's pull request. Anything but `self` still pushes and opens it, adds a "Who merges this" section to its body, arms nothing, and reports the answer in a new `mergeAuthority` field of `autoPr`, with the reason in `autoPr.remediation` and again as the step's own `remediation`: the wrap drawer shows a step's own and nothing nested under `autoPr`. It is said before the methodology's own withhold, whose instruction would be wrong here. `autoMergeWithheld` is left alone: the wrap drawer reads it as "this engine cannot run the methodology".
- `lib/wrap-steps/pr-merge.js`: the step asks after the hold check and before its push. Anything but `self` is a `skipped` result that names the pull requests and the reason; nothing is pushed onto the pull request's branch and nothing is enqueued. A project that names no coordinator runs the same commands as before and reads no remote.
- `docs/configuration-reference.md`: the `workloadNudge` row says what naming a coordinator now also means; `docs/user-guide.md`, "Wrap commits and protected branches": what such a wrap does and where its reason is shown.

**Tests.** `test/merge-authority.test.js` drives every answer through the real settings reader, registry and queue store. The two step tests gain the lane cases, with `gh` and git replaced at their existing seams. `test/auto-merge-callers.test.js` fails when a file other than the two known ones holds an arming command, or when either stops asking.

**Limits.** The wrap drawer has no sentence of its own for this case: it shows "wrap PR opened (auto-merge NOT armed)" and the reason under *How to fix this*. A project's own session can still remove its coordinator setting. The priming roll is unchanged until chunk 02, and the hold on lane wraps stays until the issue is verified after a deploy.

## 2026-10-12 — #2059: a native Codex launch is gated by its idle thread and its pane, not a status-line word

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=codex-launch-gate-2059 -->

#2059 Chunk 03. Branch `feat/2059-03-codex-launch-gate`, from `origin/main` `d3c02d6f`. The issue is an outside contributor's and stays open.

Visual change: yes (the automatic fire's row in Launch readiness reads `applied` where it read `blocked`, and its blocked sentence changes)

**Why.** A launch on Codex's native channel got its first turn only after `_awaitPaneReady` saw the text `· Ready ·` in the pane. That text is an item of a status line the operator configures, and Codex's default status line does not carry it. Measured on a signed-in 0.156.1 with the defaults (#2186 Chunk 01's probe): the pane reached an empty composer, the thread was idle, and the launch waited 90 seconds, recorded `blocked (pane_not_ready)` and sent nothing. A native launch also withholds the prime paste and the kickoff, so nothing else started the session.

**What changed.**

- `lib/startup-control-codex.js`: a new optional adapter method, `awaitLaunchReady`. Inside the same 90-second window it looks, about every 1.5 seconds, for both proofs at once: the launch's one thread is in the project directory and idle, and the pane the launch created positively shows an empty composer on the existing witness's two reads a second apart, with the thread read idle again afterwards (Architect A175 R6, R7). An idle thread alone is never ready. It reads no account, quota or trust: those are the fire's.
- **A ready gate is not a permission to send (A175 R7, R8).** The launch's automatic fire reads the pane once more (one fresh read, no second's wait) and the thread once more immediately before `turn/start`, in a trusted folder as much as one with no trust entry. A pane or thread that changed, or a read that could not be made, sends nothing: one `blocked (pane_not_ready)` row. The read sits before the row is marked `dispatching`, because the store allows no `dispatching` to `blocked` transition; one synchronous row update stands between it and the send. An operator's Fire is unchanged and is told apart by a literal `automatic: true` the fire service sets from the caller it resolved.
- **One check, three callers.** `_paneAndThread` is the pane witness followed by the thread re-read. The launch gate, the fire into a folder with no trust entry, and the send-time read all call it; `_withoutTrustEntry` keeps its own sentences and reason codes, byte for byte, and its existing tests pass unedited. It takes the channel's engine id, so a profile with no starting patterns never sets the gate ready and never sends, with the sentence that names the two fields. `_launchThread` is the thread selection lifted out of `_readiness` for the gate to share.
- **A launch with no thread yet names the dialog holding it.** Codex opens no thread while a startup dialog is up, so the gate never reached the pane and the held row could only say the thread had not opened. It now reads the pane once in that case, only to name a declared dialog and what answers it (found in the sitting, below). `_paneProof` is the pane half of `_paneAndThread`, split out for that.
- `lib/startup-control.js`: `awaitLaunchReady`, the facade. `unsupported` only when the engine's adapter does not have the method. An adapter that has it and throws, times out (the window plus 15 seconds), answers nonsense, or whose profile or session cannot be read, is `unknown`: a different value, never read as unsupported (A175 R10). The facade owns the 90-second window and hands it to the adapter, so the wait and the bound on the answer are one number and the answer is always bounded.
- `lib/launch-bootstrap.js`: a native launch asks the adapter first and uses `_awaitPaneReady` only on `unsupported`. On anything else that is not ready it fails closed with no send and the marker is never consulted, even when the pane shows it.
- `lib/startup-prompt.js`: `launchGate` builds the witness and asks the facade, so the gate and the fire read the same pane through the same profiles. The pane witness the service builds now takes `single` from the adapter and marks an answer `unavailable` when the pane could not be pinned or read, which it knows because the two tmux calls go through it. The `pane_not_ready` sentence for an adapter-proved gate gives the last thing actually observed and the Fire remedy; where the pane or the channel could not be read it says the proof was unavailable and never that the pane was seen unready (the decider's `2059c3 same code`). The marker gate's sentence is unchanged.
- `lib/pane-witness.js`: `composerShownOnce`, one read. It is `composerShown` handed a single capture for both of its reads, so no rule is written twice; `_readOnce` and `composerShown` are not edited.

**Not touched, and checked so:** `lib/sessions.js` `_awaitPaneReady`, `test/prime-readiness-gate.test.js`, `lib/medusa-wake.js`, every existing rule in `lib/pane-witness.js`. Every engine but Codex, and every legacy launch, takes the path it took.

**Limits.** A Codex status line configured down to one item has no ` · ` and is refused by the witness, as before; such a launch needs a Fire by hand. A misaligned capture is worded "the pane was not seen at an empty composer: the pane and its cursor could not be read row for row" and not as an unavailable proof: the witness gives no flag for it and its rules were not to be edited here. The legacy (non-native) Codex paste still waits on the marker; not this chunk.

**Measured, signed-in, Codex 0.156.1, Codex's default status line (2026-10-12, a scratch server from this branch, a throwaway `CODEX_HOME` holding a copy of the sign-in, deleted after 10 minutes; four one-word turns).** The automatic fire was `applied` with no hand Fire, about 9 seconds after launch: in a trusted folder (on `144d037c` and again on the tree of `755583b8`) and in a folder with no trust entry (on `144d037c`, with its note). A launch that met the update prompt was held for the whole window with nothing typed and one `blocked (pane_not_ready)` row; a hand Fire after Escape was then applied. Not measured: a resume, and a custom status line under a live automatic fire. Captures of the default, a three-item and a single-item status line were taken signed-in with the cursor and are fixtures (`composerDefaultStatusSignedIn`, `composerCustomStatus`, `composerSingleItemStatus`). Evidence is under `.prawduct/lane-evidence/2059-c03/` in the building clone, which git ignores.

**Tests.** The gate over a fake app-server and the real witness on whole captures: ready on the default and on custom status lines; each of the trust, update and opening screens, typed text, a running turn, a single-item status line, a pane that changes between reads, a thread that turns busy after the witness, a second pane, a replaced pane, another tmux server, an unreadable pane, an unanswering channel, as named refusals. The send-time read, each case in a trusted folder and in one with no entry. Fifteen guards were broken one at a time and each seen red. `test/startup-prompt-service.test.js` is outside the plan's list and was added to with the Integrator's leave, additions only.

## 2026-10-12 — #2322: the owner advances the merge queue one step at a time, and a pull request lands only when everything was read again in the call that merges

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2322-integrator-merge-queue -->

#2322 Chunk 05d. Branch `feat/2322-05d-advance-and-land`, from `origin/main` `d3c02d6f`. The issue stays open for its check after a deploy.

Visual change: no

**Why.** A queue could be made, submitted to and withdrawn from, and nothing in it could land. This chunk adds the one call that moves it, for the owner alone, with no timer: an owner that is away lands nothing.

**What changed.**

- `lib/merge-queue.js`, `advance(queue, { actor, guard, github })`: one step per call, inside the queue's slot. A `queued` entry has its candidate made (the pull request, the base tip and the base's own `test` check are read first; none is made on a red base). A `testing` entry is landed only if, in that same call, the pull request is open at the submitted head on the queue's base, the base has not moved since the candidate was made (if it has, the candidate is discarded and made again, which is not a failure), the candidate's `test` concluded success, the base's `test` is not red (and has concluded, unless the base is this queue's own landing of a tree its candidate passed on), every gate is recorded, and the comparison of base to candidate changes no release-trigger path. Then the merge, the tree comparison (the store halts the queue on a difference), and the candidate's two branches deleted. A read that fails changes nothing and answers the reader's own reason.
- A check is read by branch (`readBranchCheck`), for the base and for the candidate, and the candidate's is read before its branch is deleted.
- An entry that has or may have a candidate loses both branches before it is failed; while either is left it stays where it is. A landing is recorded first, so a landing whose branches could not be deleted is still a landing, and the next advance deletes them before it does anything else.
- A merge is asked for once. The entry is marked `landing` first; an answer that was not read is followed by a read of the pull request, and the entry is recorded as what it became. An entry found `landing` is reconciled the same way and never merged again. The landing's commit is the one the merge answered, or GitHub's `merge_commit_sha` of a pull request read as closed and merged (`lib/merge-queue-github.js#readPull` now answers it, and never an open pull request's trial merge); that exact commit is read and recorded only if it is a merge whose second parent is the submitted head (the decider's `pull merge sha yes`).
- The coordinator epoch gate and the HOLD are asked when the request arrives and again immediately before every queue write, branch deletion and the merge request, with nothing waited on between the last answer and the merge request. Once the request is sent its outcome is recorded whatever the gate would then say, and no branch is deleted for the replaced context.
- `POST /api/merge-queue/:id/clear-halt {reference}`, the Operator's alone: the store could halt a queue and no route could lift it (the decider's `halt route yes`).
- `lib/merge-queue-api.js`: `POST /api/merge-queue/advance`; a queue call the epoch gate refuses is now logged where it is thrown, with the fields every other gated mutation's refusal is logged with (submit and withdraw too).
- `tc queue advance [--queue <id>]`, waiting 180 seconds; a submit's last sentence no longer says nothing lands.
- `docs/merge-queue.md`: the procedure, what one call does, what a landing rests on and what happens when it does not hold, the four separate facts (merged, batch complete, deployed, released), and which refusals come before the epoch gate.

**Tests.** `test/merge-queue-advance.test.js` (new): the real store, GitHub faked at the calls `advance` is handed, the real `readBranchCheck` over a faked `gh` for the cases of another branch's run on the same commit. `test/merge-queue-api.test.js`: the route over HTTP, who may call it, the hold, the slot against a withdrawal both ways, each rotation state, a rotation during a landing's reads and after the merge request, and the logged refusal. `test/tc-queue-verb.test.js`, and one case of `test/tc-cli.test.js` with the Integrator's leave (the list of calls that pass a time limit gains `advance`).

**Limits.** Nothing has been landed on GitHub by this code: every test runs against a faked GitHub, and the first real landing is the check after the deploy. A merged pull request that GitHub names no merge commit for, or names one that is not a merge of the submitted head, leaves its entry `landing` with no route out. Neither browser-console command (making a queue, clearing a halt) has been typed into a browser. Which landings have had their branches deleted is remembered in the server's memory, so the first advance after a restart may repeat one harmless removal. A candidate's branches that could not be deleted are named in the answer and the server log, not in the queue's records.

## 2026-10-12 — #2323: the server reads a watched check and records its result

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2323-check-watch-wake -->

#2323 Chunk 03a. Branch `feat/2323-03a-check-watch-monitor`, from `origin/main` `250fcd72`. The issue stays open. Nobody is woken: that is chunk 03b.

Visual change: no

**Why.** A watch's record only changed when something asked, and nothing asked: a watch read `waiting` until its due time and `unknown` after it, whatever the check did. The issue's Expected 2 and 3 are that TangleClaw records the check's terminal result when it lands and that a read derives the state from that record.

**What changed.**

- New `lib/check-watch.js`: the monitor, shaped like `lib/workload-nudge.js` (`start`, `stop`, `tick`, `settled`, seams for the clock, the reader and the store). Each pass lists the open watches, reads each that is due with `github-checks#readCheck`, records the reading with `check-watch-store#recordRead`, and expires a watch that is still unresolved 24 hours past its due time. The read comes before the expiry, so a result present at the last read is the watch's result. One pass at a time; a reader that throws or answers something that is not a reading is recorded as a failed read; a pass never throws or rejects.
- **How much it reads, with nobody asking.** The timer runs a pass every 60 seconds, which sets the rate: one read per watch per minute. A 45-second guard stops a second read of one watch when passes come faster than the timer; it is shorter than the timer because a read is stamped when it completes. At most 4 reads in flight. **At most 30 reads in a pass across every project, least recently read first** (the decider's `2323 limits ok`, which kept an install-wide bound): past it the rest wait for a later pass, so the cadence stretches and no watch is starved. The log says when that starts and stops.
- `lib/check-watch-store.js`: `recordExpired(watchId)` sets `expired_at` and appends the `expired` fact in one transaction. Refused for a watch with a result, a cancelled one, an expired one, and one not yet 24 hours past due (`CHECK_WATCH_NOT_DUE`).
- `lib/check-watch-state.js`: `EXPIRE_AFTER_DUE_MS`. `derive` now says `past-due` on the two times alone, so a due time that passed is still said when only the last read's outcome cannot be made sense of (carried from chunk 01's review).
- `server.js`: the monitor is started and stopped beside the workload nudge's. Nothing else.
- `docs/check-watch.md`: a section on the monitor, the two new limits, and the opening paragraph now says results are read and nobody is woken.
- Tests: new `test/check-watch-monitor.test.js` (the real monitor over the real store on a temporary database, a fake reader, one fake clock); `test/check-watch-store.test.js` gains `recordExpired`; `test/check-watch-state.test.js` gains the `past-due` case, added with the Integrator's leave.

**What an operator sees.** A watch a session registered now reads `ready` or `failed` within about a minute of its check ending, and `unknown` with `expired` once it has gone 24 hours past due. One `gh api` call per open watch per minute is made under the server's own GitHub identity.

**Limits.** No message is sent and no pane is typed into; `monitored` stays `false` in every answer. `tc check` still ends each answer with its NOT MONITORED line, reworded: the server reads GitHub for the watch and records the result, but wakes nobody (`lib/tc-verbs.js`, one sentence, and the assertion that pins it in `test/tc-check-verb.test.js`; both added with the Integrator's leave, as were the comment on `MONITORED` in `lib/check-watch-api.js` and one test's name in `test/check-watch-api.test.js`, words only). An expiry is not held back by a last read that could not reach GitHub. Nothing bounds which repository is read: it is whatever the project folder's `origin` named.

## 2026-10-12 — #2187: the wake's pane gate refuses a dialog below a stale composer and a Codex that is still starting

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=wake-gate-2187 -->

#2187 Chunk 02. Branch `fix/2187-02-wake-pane-gate`, from `origin/main` `2adf0b93`. The issue stays open for its live check after a deploy.

Visual change: no

**Why.** Handed an idle answer by the engine's channel, the pane gate typed into three Codex 0.156.1 startup screens. That version draws on the terminal's main screen, so the empty composer it opens with stays in the read above the folder-trust and update dialogs, and the gate's text check accepted a bare prompt row anywhere in the read. The opening screen itself is an empty composer under a header that still reads `model: loading`. Chunk 01 captured all three whole and listed them as known unsafe.

**What changed.**

- `lib/medusa-wake.js`, `_assessPane`: two refusals, placed after the activity check and before anything that can answer at-prompt, the cursor on an empty composer included (the Architect's placement). (1) `_glyphRowBelowPrompt`: a row that starts with the prompt glyph below the LAST bare prompt row holds as `no-prompt`. Anchored on the last bare row because a signed-in 0.156.1 session's own history shows rows that start with the glyph above a live composer: every submitted message is drawn as one. When the cursor is on text in such a row the hold keeps `composer-has-input`. (2) `_engineStarting`: where a profile declares `headerPattern` and `startingPattern`, the NEWEST header row in the read matching the starting pattern holds as `engine-starting` (`pane-engine-starting` in the ledger and on the peer route). The dialog rule runs first, so a dialog drawn while the header still reads loading is reported as a dialog.
- One composer is excused from the first rule, as the Architect required: the switchboard's own stranded nudge, and only where the composer is seen whole to its lower border, holds exactly that nudge, and no other glyph-led row is below the last bare prompt (`_ownNudgeRegion`). Without it a nudge stranded below a stale bare row in Claude Code or Antigravity would never be cleared. Codex's composer has no border, so nothing changes there.
- The wake block gains two optional fields, declared together or not at all, each refused if it matches the empty string. They are regex sources: a header pads its value to its box's width. The name is `startingPattern`, not A123's working name `startingMarker`, because in this block a `*Pattern` is a regex compiled to `*Re` and a `*Marker` is a substring, and an existing generic test holds the block to that.
- `data/engines/codex.json` declares both under `capabilities.wake`, with evidence.
- `lib/startup-control-codex.js`: the startup fire's pane witness is handed those two patterns from the wake profile; the adapter's own two constants are gone (the decider's `2187 one declaration`). If the profile in force declares neither, the trust-free fire is held as `pane_not_ready` and the witness is not consulted. The comment on `KEY_HINT_RE` said a status row does not name keys; it can (`⚠ 1 warning · f2 to view`), and now says which key names mark a dialog footer.
- `lib/medusa-delivery-disposition.js` and the peer meanings: `pane-engine-starting` is actionable, wait.
- `docs/engine-guide.md`, `docs/configuration-reference.md`, `docs/user-guide.md`: the fields, both rules, the excused composer, and what is covered on which version.

**Two limits, stated in the guide.** (1) A usable Claude Code composer showing a pending suggestion is held as `no-prompt` when an older bare prompt row is still in the read above it: its row is not bare, so it is itself a glyph-led row below the last bare prompt. The cursor says it is empty, and would say the same of a dialog's selected option drawn dim. The Architect chose to keep the refusal (`a2187 suggestion limit`): the cost is a delayed nudge, nothing typed. The cumulative review found the frame; it is pinned. (2) Codex's header pattern is not tied to the header box, so a transcript row reading `model: loading` holds the pane as starting until it leaves the read. The witness has always read the same expressions that way.

**The profile asked for.** The adapter asks for the wake profile of the engine id the launch's channel records, which is the profile the wake reads for that session. As first written it asked for the profile called `codex`, so a profile under another id naming this adapter could have given the fire and the wake different patterns; the review found that too.

**What it does not close.** The opening screens of Codex 0.159.0 and 0.161.0 draw no `model:` header row, so the starting rule has nothing to read there, and the known-unsafe list is now exactly those two screens. A123's measurement that every opening screen carried the marker was of 0.156.1. Neither version is verified, so no session of them has a channel and no nudge reaches them. The Architect ruled this (`a2187 d4 per version`): no second starting signal is inferred here, and a positive refusal for each newer opening screen is a precondition of admitting that version in #2059.

**A test that changed on purpose.** In `test/medusa-wake-codex-fixtures.test.js` the three 0.156.1 screens leave the known-unsafe list and are pinned as held; the list's own assertion now names the two newer opening screens and checks that each is one with no header row and of an unverified version. In `test/medusa-wake-engine-activity.test.js` (added to the chunk with the Integrator's leave, for this one assertion) the settling-hold case opened with a precondition that the pane gate does not refuse the 0.156.1 loading screen: `_assessPane(LOADING_PANE, CODEX, null, true).idle === true`. That recorded the hole this chunk closes. It now asserts the refusal by its code, `{ idle: false, reason: 'engine-starting' }`. No hold assertion in the case changed: with that screen up the ledger still says `engine-thread-settling`, so the settling hold speaks first and is neither reset nor bypassed.

**Tests.** Built panes for all three engines: a selector row below a stale composer with no cursor, with the cursor on the stale row, and with the cursor on a row with no glyph; the same rows without the selector read at-prompt; a composer with only its status row; submitted messages above a live composer; a draft below a stale row; the stranded nudge excused on Claude Code and Antigravity and not when a glyph-led row sits outside its composer, when operator text follows it or when its border is unseen; Codex not excused. The starting rule: newest header decides, no header refuses nothing, the word in a transcript refuses nothing, a profile without the fields is unaffected. The captured 0.156.1 screens: each dialog held with its header removed (so by the dialog rule) and with its selector removed (so by the header), with the cursor where it was captured. The three controls together: a session released from the folder hold settles and is then refused on each startup screen, and is nudged once on a usable composer; the opening screen giving way to a composer; a turn ending. Profile validation, the disposition row, the witness's `f2` status row, and the adapter handing over the profile's own patterns.

**Evidence beyond tests.** The built gate was run over the kept snapshots of the #2186 signed-in sitting (Codex 0.156.1): every usable composer read at-prompt, after a real turn and after an interrupted one, with the captured cursor; the command-approval dialog read no-prompt in the visible pane and in the whole-history reads where the stale composer row is above it.

**Not verified.** No engine was seen live under the new gate; the hold on a real cold launch is owed after a deploy. Whether a usable Codex newer than 0.156.1 draws a glyph-led row below its composer is not established.

## 2026-10-12 — #2186: the wake no longer types into a folder Codex was told not to trust

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=codex-trust-2186 -->

#2186 Chunk 02. Branch `fix/2186-02-wake-untrusted-folder-hold`, from `origin/main` `2a9b9741`. The issue stays open: the signed-in automatic launch and the live-install checks remain.

Visual change: no

**Why.** The startup fire refuses to send into a folder whose entry in Codex's config says anything but trusted. The wake did not look: its observation skipped trust by design. A signed-in probe on Codex 0.156.1 (a scratch server, a stand-in for the switchboard bridge, a throwaway Codex home) showed the result: in a folder with `trust_level = "untrusted"` the wake typed its nudge 11 to 16 seconds after the mail, in the same session whose fire had just been refused, and Codex started a turn on it and asked to run a command. The same happened when the entry was added while the session ran. The wake was a way round the fire's refusal.

**What changed.**

- `lib/startup-control-codex.js`: `observeActivity` reads `config/read` on the connection it already holds, only when it is about to answer `idle`, and answers `held` / `folder-untrusted` for a readable entry that is not trusted (`_folderHeld`). No entry and a `null` table do not hold. A read that fails, or an answer with no readable projects table, does not hold either and is warned about once per launch: it establishes nothing about trust. Busy and unknown answers make no extra request. The channel-generation re-read still guards every idle and held answer.
- `lib/startup-control.js`: the facade admits `held` beside idle, busy and unknown; any other state is still rewritten to unknown. The proposal sent to the Architect missed this layer: it was written from the adapter and the wake and not from what sits between them, where `held` would have been rewritten to the stall code. Found on reading the facade before writing the wake's arm; the Integrator gave leave for the file.
- `lib/medusa-wake.js`: a `held` / `folder-untrusted` answer holds as `engine-folder-untrusted`, named before the settling hold, which it neither starts nor resets. A `held` answer with any other reason holds as unknown. One peer meaning.
- `lib/medusa-delivery-disposition.js`: the code is `configuration` with a new next action, `review-folder-trust` (the Architect's name and wording): the recipient's operator may set the entry to trusted to resume automatic wakes, or leave it and read the mail by hand.
- `docs/engine-guide.md` states the rule for all four cases, who decided it, what a failed read does and does not mean, and what was measured. `docs/user-guide.md` lists the new reason.

**Decided by.** The policy is the decider's ruling of 2026-10-11 (hold on an explicit non-trusted entry, not on a missing one; an unreadable config keeps mail moving and is logged). The Architect cleared the design on 2026-10-12 with four requirements, all built: the once-per-launch warning; no recall of a nudge already typed, its ledger row kept; a held answer on an already-nudged edge recorded as a readiness observation only, with a test through the held-to-trusted transition and a re-arm; the rule applied to every version the channel's version gate admits, with no version added to `remoteTrustPrompt.absentOn`.

**A test that changed on purpose.** `test/startup-control-codex.test.js` asserted that an observation never calls `config/read`. That was the behaviour being replaced. The case now asserts no turn, account or quota call, and new cases assert exactly one `config/read` per idle observation and none for a busy or unknown one.

**Tests.** The adapter: untrusted and other non-trusted values hold; trusted, no entry, another folder's entry and a null table do not; a failing read and an unreadable table are idle and warned once per launch, again for a new launch; no read for busy, unknown or a working second thread; the answer follows an entry changed mid-session; a held answer is withheld when the channel changed under the read. The facade: `held` passes through; an out-of-vocabulary state does not. The wake: nothing typed however long; recorded once per edge; no stall alert; the peer route's meaning; trusted releases with no relaunch and settles like any first idle; a settled thread is not made to settle again; a nudge already typed is not recalled; three observe-only cases; the recorded settling sample still held. The disposition: class, next action and its sentence. Each new guard was broken in turn and a test failed.

**Not verified.** No Codex version but 0.156.1 was probed. What Codex does when the approval it asked for is given was not measured. The hold was not seen on a live install: it is proved by tests against the fake app-server, and by the probe of the behaviour it replaces. A folder holding its own `.codex/` config was not probed.

## 2026-10-12 — #2322: a context the owner's rotation has replaced can no longer submit to or withdraw from the merge queue

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=2322-integrator-merge-queue -->

#2322 Chunk 05c. Branch `feat/2322-05c-rotation-fence`, from `origin/main` `2a9b9741`. The issue stays open. It closes a gap in what chunk 04 merged; the queue still lands nothing.

Visual change: no

**Why.** Launch binding proves the owner's launch is live. It does not prove that the engine thread making the call is the one the owner's coordinator rotation bound, so after a managed context rotation the replaced context, in the same live launch, could still submit and withdraw. The Architect ruled the fence (`2322 epoch fence`, A177 R1, queue Rulings item 30; the decider concurred).

**What changed.**

- `lib/coordinator-rotation.js`: `merge-queue-mutate` is a gated action. The gate has no branch for it, so it is judged as `control-mutate` is: refused while `fenced`, `rebinding` or `reconciling` (the bound replacement included), accepted while `active` only from the bound replacement, and not judged for a project that never rotated or whose rotation was abandoned.
- `lib/merge-queue-api.js`: `submit` asks the gate after the owner check and again after the GitHub reads, immediately before the entry is written. `withdraw` asks at entry, immediately before the candidate's branches are deleted, and again immediately before the entry is recorded withdrawn. No check is separated from its step by a wait. The gate is given the owner project, the caller's verified launch with its session (`lib/shared-docs-access.js#resolveAccess`) and the `x-tangleclaw-engine-thread` header; its refusal reaches the caller with its own status, code and details. The gate is a seam (`_internal.rotationGate`) as the GitHub calls are.
- A rotation that begins while a withdrawal is deleting branches leaves the entry in the queue with its branches gone; the replacement context withdraws again, which is harmless.
- Not gated: the two reads, and the Operator's two routes. `NOT_OWNER` and the launch binding are answered before the gate.
- `docs/merge-queue.md`: the limit "a coordinator in a context rotation is not fenced" is removed; a section says what each rotation state does to a submit and a withdrawal, and the two codes join the refusal table.
- `docs/coordinator-rotation.md`: one bullet in its list of the mutations the gate judges (added to this chunk's files with the Integrator's leave).
- Tests: `test/coordinator-rotation.test.js` holds the new word to `control-mutate`'s verdict for six callers in each state; `test/merge-queue-api.test.js` covers both calls over HTTP in each state, a stale thread of the same launch, a call with no thread header, a lapsed epoch, a rotation beginning during submit's reads and during withdraw's deletion, and the place of each of the five asks. `withCandidate` moved up one scope in that file, unchanged, so the new block can use it.

**Limits.** `tc` forwards the thread only where the engine sets `CODEX_THREAD_ID`. No server route was changed (`server.js` untouched). No deployment; who owns a queue is unchanged. `advance` does not exist yet and is built already fenced.

## 2026-10-12 — #2322: a queue call that is slow is not called unreachable, a withdrawal's reason is checked first, and one withdrawal runs per queue

<!-- prawduct: type=bugfix | scope=2322-integrator-merge-queue -->

#2322 Chunk 05b. Branch `feat/2322-05b-queue-call-limits`, from `origin/main` `8ee3832a`. The issue stays open. It repairs three things in what chunk 04 merged; the queue still lands nothing.

Visual change: no

**Why.**

- `bin/tc` gave every request 10 seconds and then said the API was unreachable. A `tc queue submit` makes the server read GitHub four or more times, each read bounded at 10 seconds, so with GitHub slow the pane was told the API was unreachable while the server went on to queue the entry.
- `withdraw` deleted a candidate's branches and only then handed the reason to the store, which refuses one over 1000 characters: branches gone for a withdrawal that was not recorded.
- `withdraw` reads an entry's state, waits on GitHub and then writes, so a second call on the same queue could run in between.

**What changed.**

- `bin/tc`: `requestJson` takes `opts.timeoutMs`; with none it waits the 10 seconds it always did. The rule is `requestTimeoutMs` in `lib/tc-verbs.js`. A wait that ran out, as opposed to a connection that failed, is marked on the error (`timedOut`, `timeoutMs`); its message and code are unchanged.
- **The calls that pass a limit are two, and no other:** `tc queue submit` (`POST /api/merge-queue/entries`) and `tc queue withdraw` (`POST /api/merge-queue/entries/:id/withdraw`), 60 seconds each (`QUEUE_CALL_TIMEOUT_MS`). Every other verb, the two queue reads and `bin/tc`'s own HOLD banner pass none.
- `lib/tc-verbs.js`: a `tc queue` call whose wait ran out says, for a submit or a withdrawal, that the request may still be running and to read `tc queue status` before repeating; for `status` or `show`, to repeat it. Exit 2, and never the unreachable text.
- New `lib/merge-queue.js`: `withQueueSlot(queueId, fn)`, one slot per queue id in the server's memory, taken before the function first yields and given back in a `finally`. A second call is refused, not queued.
- `lib/merge-queue-api.js#withdraw`: the reason is checked for the store's limit before anything else; the rest runs inside the queue's slot, and a withdrawal that finds it taken is refused `409 QUEUE_CALL_IN_FLIGHT` having changed nothing. `submit` does not take the slot.
- `docs/merge-queue.md`: the two waits, what to do when one runs out, the reason checked first, one at a time per queue, the new refusal.
- Tests: new `test/merge-queue-slot.test.js`; cases added to `test/merge-queue-api.test.js`, `test/tc-queue-verb.test.js` and `test/tc-cli.test.js`. The last spawns the real `tc` against a server that answers a submit after 11 seconds and nothing else, which takes that long to run.

**What an operator sees.** A `tc queue submit` or `withdraw` that takes longer than 10 seconds now answers, up to 60; past that the pane is told the request may still be running, not that the API is unreachable.

**Limits.** The slot is memory only: a restart frees it, which is safe because a withdrawal is harmless to repeat. Nothing else takes the slot until `advance` exists. The 1000 is written in `lib/merge-queue-api.js` as well as in the store, which does not export it; a test holds the two to the same number.

## 2026-10-12 — #2321: one command writes down what CI and the local complement covered for a commit, or refuses

<!-- prawduct: type=feature | scope=2321-ci-as-suite-evidence -->

#2321 Chunk 02b. Branch `feat/2321-02b-ci-evidence`, from `origin/main` `4a3dd268`. The last chunk of this issue's build. The issue stays open: the lane rule it asks for is not in force.

Visual change: no
